# Parity checklist — the behavioural reference

The **web app is the reference** (SPEC.md). This file, together with the Playwright flows in
`web/e2e/`, is what any native port is built and checked against. Every PR that adds or changes a
screen updates its entry here — describe behaviour, not Vue code, so a port does not have to read
`.vue` files to know what a screen does.

Rules for a port, if one happens:
- Match the web behaviour exactly. No extra features, no redesign. Native idioms are allowed where the
  platform demands them (navigation stack, sheets, haptics), and noted in the entry.
- Same names across platforms: `app/data/` repositories, stores ↔ ViewModels
  (`useLibraryStore` ↔ `LibraryViewModel`).
- IDs: the web `data-testid` is the native `accessibilityIdentifier` (`<screen>.<element>`).
- Copy: the keys in `web/i18n/locales/en.json` are the keys of the native string catalogues
  (`Localizable.strings`, `strings.xml`). Entries name keys, not sentences.
- An entry is done on a platform only when its flow passes there (Playwright on web, Maestro native).

## Entry template

```
### <Screen name>  (web: `app/pages/<path>.vue`)
Purpose: one sentence.
Layout: what is on the screen, top to bottom.
States: loading · empty · content · error · offline (list what exists)
Actions → result:
- <action> → <what happens, incl. navigation and what the database is asked to do>
Edge cases: validation, limits, failures, offline
Copy keys: <screen>.<key>, …
IDs: <screen>.<element>, …
Flow: `web/e2e/<flow>.spec.ts`
- [ ] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android
```

## Screens

### Sign in  (web: `app/pages/sign-in.vue`)
Purpose: the way back in for a member; one field, the address.
Layout: the way-in frame (`components/auth/Frame.vue`, docs/DESIGN.md): a wall of cloth covers behind a veil, the wordmark and tagline, the screen's eyebrow title, the form, the link to the other screen at the bottom. Same frame on Sign up and Verify, in both themes; the theme is the device's or the one chosen in the Profile (see Theme).
States: form · busy (submit disabled) · error (inline under the field, the field's rule turns red) · config problem (the build has no Supabase URL/key; replaces the form)
Actions → result:
- Submit address → asks Supabase Auth for a six-digit code for that address **without creating an account** (`createAuth.requestCode`). Known address: the address is stored as the *pending sign-in* (see Verify) and the app goes to Verify. Unknown address: not an error, the app goes to Sign up with the address prefilled.
- "Sign up" link (`signIn.signUpLink`) → Sign up (address field empty).
Edge cases: a malformed address is refused by the field (browser validation) or by the backend (`email_invalid`); asking for another mail within a second is `rate_limited`. A signed-in member opening this screen is sent to Home. A signed-out launch with a pending address is sent to Verify instead.
Copy keys: `app.name`, `app.tagline`, `signIn.title`, `signIn.intro`, `signIn.emailLabel`, `signIn.emailPlaceholder`, `signIn.submit`, `signIn.noAccount`, `signIn.signUpLink`, `signIn.configMissing`, `signIn.configInvalidUrl`, `auth.error.*`
IDs: `signIn.brand`, `signIn.title`, `signIn.email`, `signIn.error`, `signIn.submit`, `signIn.signUp`, `signIn.configProblem`
Flow: `web/e2e/auth.spec.ts` (every flow starts here)
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Sign up  (web: `app/pages/sign-up.vue`)
Purpose: a new member joins with their address and an invite code. Libellus is invite-only.
Layout: the way-in frame (see Sign in).
States: form · busy · error under the invite field (`invite_*`) or under the address field (anything else)
Actions → result:
- Submit address + invite code → the invite is pre-flighted (`invite_code_status` answers valid / invalid / expired / exhausted / missing), then Supabase Auth creates the account and mails a six-digit code (`createAuth.requestSignUpCode`); the address is stored as the pending sign-in and the app goes to Verify. Nothing is spent yet.
- "Sign in" link → Sign in.
Edge cases: the database gate is authoritative (a trigger on account creation refuses a missing, unknown, expired or used-up code); the pre-flight only tells the form why. The invite is **consumed when the address is proved** (Verify), and judged again under a lock: if the last use went to someone else in between, Verify fails with `invite_gone`. Codes are case-insensitive and trimmed. Signing up with an address that already has an account just mails it a sign-in code and costs no invite. Addresses that never get proved are removed after a day.
Copy keys: `app.name`, `app.tagline`, `signUp.title`, `signUp.intro`, `signUp.emailLabel`, `signUp.emailPlaceholder`, `signUp.inviteLabel`, `signUp.invitePlaceholder`, `signUp.submit`, `signUp.hasAccount`, `signUp.signInLink`, `auth.error.invite_required|invite_invalid|invite_expired|invite_exhausted`
IDs: `signUp.brand`, `signUp.title`, `signUp.email`, `signUp.emailError`, `signUp.inviteCode`, `signUp.inviteError`, `signUp.submit`, `signUp.signIn`
Flow: `web/e2e/auth.spec.ts` (sign up with the dev invite; an invalid invite is refused under its field)
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Verify  (web: `app/pages/verify.vue`)
Purpose: type the six-digit code from the mail. Typed, never a link, so it works inside the installed iOS PWA.
Layout: the way-in frame (see Sign in); six drawn cells, the next one lit with the lamp caret; Resend is a quiet pill, "Use another email" the link at the bottom.
States: waiting for the code · busy · error (under the code, all cells red) · resend cooling down (60 s after each mail; the pill is dimmed and counts down)
Actions → result:
- Type the sixth digit → submits (no button). Success: the member is signed in, the pending address is dropped, the app goes to Home. Failure: the error shows, the field clears and keeps the keyboard.
- Resend → mails a new code to the pending address (the sign-in call, in both flows) and restarts the 60 s cooldown.
- "Use another email" → drops the pending address, goes to Sign in.
Edge cases: **the pending address is persisted for one hour** (`libellus.pendingSignIn` in local storage: address, flow, time sent), so iOS killing the installed app while the member reads Mail does not lose it: a signed-out launch with a pending address opens Verify, and Verify without one goes to Sign in. After an hour it is discarded (the code expired too). One input, not six: the cells are only a drawing, so the iOS code suggestion and paste work (`autocomplete="one-time-code"`, numeric keyboard, digits only). The mail it waits for is designed in the app's own look, light and dark (`supabase/templates/magic_link.html`, generated: docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, Emails); the six digits are one run of text, which is what the e2e mailbox helper reads. A wrong, expired or already-used code is one error (`code_invalid`): Supabase deliberately cannot tell them apart. `invite_gone` when the invite ran out between Sign up and now. `rate_limited` when resend is tapped twice.
Copy keys: `app.name`, `app.tagline`, `verify.title`, `verify.sentTo`, `verify.codeLabel`, `verify.resend`, `verify.resendIn`, `verify.changeEmail`, `auth.error.code_invalid|invite_gone|rate_limited|unknown`
IDs: `verify.brand`, `verify.title`, `verify.sentTo`, `verify.code`, `verify.error`, `verify.resend`, `verify.changeEmail`
Flow: `web/e2e/auth.spec.ts` (the code read from Mailpit; a mistyped code; the pending address survives a relaunch)
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Tab shell  (web: `app/layouts/tabs.vue`, `components/shell/*`; pages `app/pages/{index,library}.vue`)
Purpose: the signed-in frame: each tab's header with the avatar, the page, the floating tab bar, and the search overlay that opens over all of it. Home is described in its own section below. A pushed screen (the book page) lives in the same frame without the header (see Book).
Layout: a page header on top (`shell.header`, iOS's Large Title bar: under the safe area a 44 pt row with only the avatar at the right, then 10 below it the title — the date eyebrow over the greeting on Home, the large title on Library — and 10 under that the page), the page, and over the page's bottom edge the floating tab bar (`shell.tabs`: Home, Library, Search as a capsule). The search overlay replaces the tab bar with the palette (see Search); the avatar opens the Profile (see Profile). Wide screens: the same column, centred.
States: content (each tab shows its empty state) · search overlay closed / open (keyboard up: "typing", or down)
Actions → result:
- Tap Home or Library in the tab bar → shows Home (`/`) or Library (`/library`), scrolled to where that tab was left (from the tab bar, the search palette's tabs or any link; a tab never left, or entered after signing in again, opens at its top). Back and forward return to the place the browser saved for that entry. A place deeper than the page is tall when the router scrolls (a slow device, the content still coming in) is landed on as soon as the page has grown to it, for at most 2 s (`PLACE_PATIENCE`; then as far down as the page goes), and not at all once the member scrolls, taps or types herself meanwhile (`untilReachable` in `utils/tabPlaces.ts`); until the page is in its place the document carries `data-moving`. The current tab is full ink with a lamp dot and its `aria-current="page"`; the others are faint. The header shows the page's title: Home under a date eyebrow (`home.today`, e.g. "Friday · 2 Oct"), Library large on its own.
- Tap the tab already showing → it scrolls back to its top (smoothly; at once with Reduce Motion); nothing navigates. Native: each tab's own navigation stack keeps its place.
- Tap Search in the tab bar, or the search prompt in an empty state → the **search overlay** opens over the current page; **the URL does not change** and the page stays behind it, blurred and veiled. The query field has the keyboard ("typing"): the bottom row is the search icon, the field (placeholder `search.placeholder`), a clear button once something is typed, and **Cancel**. With the keyboard down the row shows Home and Library at its left (the current one lit) instead of Cancel; tapping one goes there and closes the overlay. Above the row are the results (see Search).
- Close the overlay → Cancel (while typing), a tap on the page behind it (`search.backdrop`), swiping the palette down (more than 80 px, or a quick flick), Escape, or going to another page (the palette turns back into the capsule, whose Home and Library return only once the flying Search icon has passed them) (a navigation that started while it was open; a tab tapped just before Search whose page is still loading arrives behind the open search instead of closing it). The query is dropped; the page underneath is as it was.
- Tap the avatar (the initials of her name when she gave one: "Fabian" → `F`, "Ida Tester" → `IT`; otherwise of the address: `ida.tester@example.com` → `IT`, `ida@example.com` → `ID`) → opens the **Profile** (`/profile`, a pushed screen; see Profile), which holds the account: the address, **Name** (see Member name), the **Dark mode** switch, **Import books**, **Sign out**. (Until #78 these lived in a small avatar menu.)
- Dark mode switch (in the Profile) → see Theme below. On when dark is showing.
- Scroll any page of the shell → once something has scrolled under the status bar, a thin veil of the surface colour (`shell.scrollEdge`: solid over the top inset, fading out `ms` (12) below it, no blur, not under a pushed screen's top bar; in a browser tab, where the inset is 0, only the 12 px fade) keeps what runs under the clock from crowding it; at the top of the page it is not there, so a book's light shows. It never takes a tap. Native: the platform's own scroll edge effect.
- Sign out (in the Profile) → ends the session on the device and revokes it on the server, removes everything the device cached under the `libellus.` prefix (the pending address, the device's copy of the Library: see Offline and install), goes to Sign in. The theme preference is not under that prefix and stays.
Every navigation is said: a polite live region reads the new document title (`NuxtRouteAnnouncer` in `app.vue`; a native port moves its screen reader's focus to the new screen). Edge cases: the session is restored on launch before the first route resolves, so a signed-in member never sees Sign in flash past. Signed-out members opening any route are sent to Sign in (to Verify if a code is pending); signed-in members opening Sign in / Sign up / Verify are sent Home. A session that ends elsewhere (another tab, a failed refresh) lands on Sign in too. In dev builds, routes starting with `/prototype` skip the guard. There is no `/search` route any more (search is never a page; nothing deep-links to it yet): it is an unknown route like any other. Wide screens: the same layout in a centred column with hairlines left and right; the tab bar and the overlay stay centred. Header, tab bar and overlay respect the device's edges (Device edges, below); with the keyboard up the overlay sits right above it. Native: the overlay is a sheet-like panel over the current view, not a pushed screen. Web on iOS: a passive no-op `touchstart` listener on the document lets Safari apply the press styles (`:active`).
Copy keys: `app.name`, `tabs.home|library|search`, `home.greeting.*|today|emptyTitle|empty`, `library.title|emptyTitle|empty`, `search.title|placeholder|close|clear|empty`, `common.cancel`, `shell.tabsLabel`, `shell.avatarLabel` (the account's copy is the Profile's, see Profile)
IDs: `shell.header`, `shell.avatar`, `profile.email`, `profile.name`, `profile.nameValue`, `profile.theme`, `profile.signOut`, `shell.tabs`, `shell.tab.home|library|search`, `home.date`, `home.title` (the greeting), `home.emptyTitle`, `home.empty`, `home.search`, `library.title`, `library.emptyTitle`, `library.empty`, `library.search`, `search.overlay`, `search.backdrop`, `search.query`, `search.clear`, `search.cancel`, `search.tab.home|library`, `search.empty`
Flow: `web/tests/tab-places.test.ts` (each tab opens where it was left, the current tab tapped again goes to its top, back returns to the saved place; the places, and the wait for a page tall enough), `web/e2e/auth.spec.ts` (sign up → Home → Library → a reload keeps the session → the Search tab opens the overlay over Library and Cancel closes it → avatar → the Profile's Sign out → signed out again). The tab bar's morph into the palette has no flow (motion); the palette's geometry is in `web/tests/keyboard.test.ts`
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Theme  (web: `app/utils/theme.ts`, `app/stores/theme.ts`, the switch in `components/profile/Account.vue`)
Purpose: light (D's Day) or dark (D's Night). Follows the phone until the member chooses (issue #1, story 70).
Layout: one switch row (`role=switch`), `profile.account.theme`, in the Profile's account rows between Name and Import books; nothing else on screen changes except the colours.
States: no preference (follows the device, live) · light · dark. The switch shows two states only: on = dark is showing.
Actions → result:
- First tap on the switch → stores **the opposite of what is showing**: on a dark phone with no preference, Light. Every later tap flips between Light and Dark. There is no way back to "follow the phone" from the interface.
- The stored choice → `<html data-theme>`, both `theme-color` tags (browser chrome, installed app's status bar) take the theme's surface colour; with no choice there is no attribute and each `theme-color` tag answers its own `prefers-color-scheme`.
Edge cases: stored on the device in local storage (`libellus-theme`), never in Supabase, not cleared by sign-out, so the way in looks as the member left it. Applied before the first paint by an inline script at the top of the HTML, so a launch never flashes the other theme. Unreadable or unknown stored values count as no preference; storage that refuses writes still flips the theme for the visit. Native: the same preference in the platform's user defaults, applied as the window's interface style; `null` = unspecified.
Copy keys: `profile.account.theme`
IDs: `profile.theme`
Flow: no flow; the rule in `web/tests/theme.test.ts`
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Member name  (web: `components/shell/NameSheet.vue`, the Name row in `components/profile/Account.vue`, `setName` / `memberOf` / `cleanName` in `data/auth.ts`, `stores/session.ts`, `composables/useGreeting.ts`, `utils/initials.ts`)
Purpose: an optional first name, so Home greets her as D does ("Good evening, Fabian") and the avatar shows her initials (issue #49, audit item 10).
Layout: the Profile's **Name** row (`account.name`, pencil icon) with the name at the right, or `account.nameNone` ("Add") in the ghost colour. It opens the sheet `accountName` (`UiSheet`, title `account.nameTitle`, **Save** at the top right): one field `account.nameLabel` (placeholder `account.namePlaceholder`, at most 40 characters, autocomplete `given-name`), the hint `account.nameHint`, and, while she has a name, **Remove name** (`account.clear`).
States: no name · a name · saving (the field and buttons disabled) · error (`account.error.offline|rate_limited|unknown` under the field; the sheet stays) · offline (Save and Remove name read "Offline", disabled).
Actions → result:
- Tap Name → the sheet rises over the Profile with the field focused once the sheet has finished rising (the keyboard comes up then, not mid-rise) and holding the current name.
- Save (or Enter; disabled while the cleaned field equals the current name) → **one call**, Supabase Auth `updateUser({ data: { name } })`: the name is kept in the account's own metadata (`user_metadata.name`), cleaned first (`cleanName`: trimmed, inner runs of spaces made one, cut at 40 characters; nothing left = no name, stored as null). No table, no migration, no RLS: only the signed-in member can change her own account. The sheet closes; the Profile's name and ring, the greeting and the avatar change at once.
- Remove name, or Save with the field empty → the same call with null: back to the plain greeting and the address's initials.
- Cancel, the scrim, Escape, a swipe down → nothing changes.
Edge cases: every member read carries the name (`memberOf`: `{ id, email, name? }`, no `name` key when there is none), so a reload, another device and an offline start (the device's copy of the Library keeps it with the member: `SavedMember.name`) greet her the same way. Refused offline before anything is sent (`offline`). A member without a session cannot set one.
Copy keys: `account.*`, `home.greeting.morningNamed|afternoonNamed|eveningNamed`, `common.cancel`, `common.offline`
IDs: `profile.name`, `profile.nameValue`, `accountName`, `accountName.cancel`, `accountName.scrim`, `accountName.action`, `accountName.input`, `accountName.error`, `accountName.clear`
Flow: no flow (not a critical path); the repository and the cleaning in `web/tests/member-name.test.ts`, the initials in `web/tests/initials.test.ts`
Native: the same metadata field through the platform's Supabase client; a sheet with one text field.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Profile photo  (web: `components/profile/Photo.vue` in `app/pages/profile/index.vue`, the Photo row of `components/profile/Account.vue`, the ring of `components/profile/Hero.vue`, `components/ui/Avatar.vue` in `components/shell/Header.vue`, `stores/avatar.ts`, `data/avatar.ts`, `utils/avatarRaster.ts`, `utils/crop.ts`, `composables/useCrop.ts`, the `avatars` store under `data/localData.ts`; database: `supabase/migrations/20261009120000_avatars.sql` — the bucket `avatars`, its policies, `accounts.avatar_path|avatar_updated_at`, `set_avatar`, `delete_my_account`)
Purpose: the member's own photo in her avatars instead of her initials (issue #156). Shown to her, and to the members connected to her (see the last Edge case and Opening the app below).
Layout: the Profile's **Photo** row (`photo.row`, camera icon) first in the account rows, with her small photo at the right or `photo.rowNone` ("Add"); the hero's ring is a button for the same (`photo.avatarLabel`). The sheet `photo` (`UiSheet`) in two states. **Her photo** (title `photo.title`): the photo in the hero's ring, `photo.private` (who sees it: the members connected to her, and everyone in Libellus while her account is public; saved small, without where or when it was taken), **Choose another** (`photo.replace`; without a photo `photo.choose`, primary) and **Remove photo** (`photo.remove`, danger). **The crop** (title `photo.cropTitle`, "Move and scale", **Save** at the top right): the picked picture in a square under a round mask, − / slider / + for the zoom (1× to 4×), the hint `photo.hint`. Every avatar (`UiAvatar` in each tab's header, the hero, the row) draws the photo over the initials in the same ring. Other members' avatars (`components/friends/Avatar.vue`: the feed, the batch sheet, People, a member's profile) draw their photo the same way, the 128 px file in rows and the 512 px one in a hero, and the initials when the card has no photo.
States: no photo (initials, "Add") · a photo · the crop · saving (`photo.saving`, Save disabled) · removing (`photo.removing`) · error under the content (`photo.error.unreadable|offline|too_large|not_signed_in|missing|unknown`; the sheet stays) · offline (the row is disabled and says "Offline", the hero's ring is disabled, the sheet's actions say "Offline") · saved / removed (said once in a polite status, `photo.saved|removed`).
Actions → result:
- Tap the row or the hero's ring without a photo → the device's picker opens in the same tap (`<input type="file" accept="image/*">`: images only; phones offer the camera and the library). With a photo → the sheet with it; Choose another opens the picker.
- A picture is picked → decoded upright (the browser applies the EXIF orientation) → the crop at 1×, the largest square in the middle. A file that cannot be decoded (`unreadable`) → the sheet with the error, nothing sent.
- Drag (one finger, the mouse) moves the picture; two fingers pinch; the wheel, the slider, − and + zoom around the square's centre; the square is focusable (`photo.area`, a labelled group described by the hint): arrows move it (Shift: four times as far), + and − zoom. The picture always covers the square (`utils/crop.ts`: `clampPan`, `zoomAround`, `cropRect`).
- Save → on the device: the square cut out of the original in a canvas, scaled down in halves to **512 px** and **128 px** (never upscaled: a smaller cut-out keeps its own size), encoded as **WebP** (JPEG for both where the browser cannot encode WebP: Safari), the quality stepped down from 0.86 until each file is ≤ 100 kB, and every metadata segment (EXIF with GPS, XMP, ICC, IPTC, comments) stripped (`encodeAvatar`, `stripMetadata`). Then **two uploads and one call**: `avatars/<her id>/<hash>.webp` and `<hash>-128.webp` (hash: the first 32 hex digits of the large file's SHA-256), then `set_avatar(path)`, which checks the path's shape and that both files are there, sets `accounts.avatar_path` and `avatar_updated_at`, and returns the path before; then the rest of her folder (the old photo, anything a broken upload left) is deleted through the Storage API. The sheet closes and every avatar shows the new photo.
- Remove photo → `set_avatar(null)`, then her folder is emptied; the initials are back everywhere.
- Cancel, the scrim, Escape, a swipe down (outside the square) or Back → nothing changes.
- Opening the app → each avatar shows the photo the device keeps (IndexedDB, `libellus` database, store `avatars`: the path and both files), offline too; online, `accounts.avatar_path` is read once per sign-in and the files downloaded with her session only when the path is a new one. A path whose files are gone (a restored backup has no Storage) shows the initials. Other members' photos: a card's `photo` is shown only when it is not null; the device keeps one record per member in the same `avatars` store (the path and the 128 and 512 px files), replaces it when the card's path changes, deletes it when the card's photo is null (blocked, removed, private), downloads nothing offline, and loses all of it on sign-out.
Edge cases: the bucket is **private** (no public URL; 256 kB a file, WebP and JPEG only): she reads, lists, writes and deletes her own folder only, with the path's shape `<her id>/<32 hex>[-128].webp|jpg`; another member and a signed-out caller can do none of that (`supabase/tests/avatars_test.sql`). Signing out deletes the device's copy with the rest. Who sees her photo: `can_see_member_photo` (the avatars select policy for followers, and for any member while the account is public); a member she blocks, or who is not connected to her, gets no photo. Deleting the account empties her folder first (Storage files only go through the Storage API) and `delete_my_account` refuses (`photo_remains`) while a file is left. Not in the nightly database backup (docs/OPERATIONS.md, Backups).
Copy keys: `photo.*`, `common.offline`, `common.cancel`
IDs: `profile.photo`, `profile.photoValue`, `profile.avatar`, `profile.avatarPhoto`, `photo`, `photo.cancel`, `photo.action`, `photo.scrim`, `photo.sheetTitle`, `photo.file`, `photo.status`, `photo.preview`, `photo.choose`, `photo.remove`, `photo.area`, `photo.picture`, `photo.zoom`, `photo.zoomIn`, `photo.zoomOut`, `photo.error`; `data-avatar-photo` on an avatar's photo
Flow: no flow (not a critical path); the geometry, the pipeline and the repository in `web/tests/avatar.test.ts`; the bucket and its policies in `supabase/tests/avatars_test.sql`
Native: the same bucket, paths, sizes and `set_avatar` through the platform's Supabase client; the platform's photo picker and its own crop; encode with the platform's image API (it writes no EXIF when given only pixels) and keep the files in the app's own storage.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Sheets and confirmations  (web: `components/ui/Sheet.vue`, `components/ui/Confirm.vue`, `composables/useModalLayer.ts`, `composables/useBackDismiss.ts`, `composables/useKeyboardInset.ts`, `utils/keyboard.ts`)
Purpose: what every bottom sheet and every confirmation does to the rest of the app, as on iOS (issue #49, audit items 11–14).
Behaviour:
- **Out of reach behind it.** While a sheet or a confirmation is open every other part of the app is `inert` (the page, the tab bar, the search palette, a sheet under a confirmation): no tap, no Tab, no VoiceOver reaches it. The page does not scroll under it (the root's `overflow` is hidden, the sheet's own list does not hand its scroll on to the page, a drag on the scrim does nothing). Closing the top one gives the one under it back (a confirmation over the read's edit sheet → the sheet again; the page stays shut until the sheet closes too).
- **Focus.** It moves into the sheet as it opens (to the panel at once, then to the field a sheet marks to have the keyboard: the Collection name, the member name, once the sheet has finished rising, so the keyboard does not arrive mid-rise; not if the member has already put focus elsewhere in the sheet; at once with Reduce Motion or for a sheet that is put back; `web/app/utils/sheetFocus.ts` holds the one switch for iOS). It goes back to what opened it when it closes, if that is still there and focus was still in the sheet; a sheet that opened another (Update progress → Finish) hands its opener on.
- **The keyboard (Android).** Chrome resizes the page to what the keyboard leaves (`interactive-widget=resizes-content` in the viewport meta, `nuxt.config.ts`), as an Android app's window resizes: the sheet and the search palette stand on the keyboard by themselves (the palette `floatAbove` over it), a sheet's height shrinks with `100dvh` so its header stays in view, and the keyboard inset reads 0, so nothing is lifted twice. Without it Chrome laid the keyboard over the page and panned the page up to the focused field, which pushed the Finish sheet's header off the top.
- **The keyboard (iOS).** With the keyboard up the sheet rides on it (`translateY` by the keyboard's height less the home-indicator padding, on the keyboard curve: `keyboardInsetOf` against the layout viewport, the larger of `innerHeight` and the root's `clientHeight`: `layoutHeightOf`; `sheetLift`), its height shrinks so its top stays on screen, and the focused field scrolls into view inside the sheet (`revealDelta`, the `md` margin). The same keyboard height the search palette follows (`useKeyboardInset`; the palette sits `--spacing-sm` above it, `paletteLift`).
- **Back (#62).** The system Back — Android's back gesture and button, the browser's Back, iOS's edge swipe in a Safari tab — closes the top-most open layer and stays on the page: a confirmation before the sheet under it, then the sheet; the search the same way. Back again leaves the page. Each open layer has a history entry of its own over the page's (same address); closing it any other way (Cancel, the scrim, a swipe, Escape, the action) takes the entry off again, so no Back is spent on something already gone, and one sheet handing over to another (Update progress → Finish) keeps the one entry. A confirmation that is running its action does not close (its entry goes back). A part of the page that keeps an entry of its own above a sheet's (Regal's row in Home's *Read in* sheet, a Book broken out) answers a Back off it itself: the router lets it pass, the sheet stays, and the next Back closes it. **Any change of page closes every sheet and confirmation** (a sheet never stays over the next page; before the change their entries come off, so the history is what it would be without them); the search keeps its own rule (it closes for a page change that started while it was open).
- **The status bar under the scrim (Android, A6).** In the installed app (`display: standalone`) the system draws the status bar outside the web viewport and colours it from the `theme-color` tags, so the scrim, a layer inside the viewport, cannot dim it. While a sheet or a confirmation is on screen (open, or still leaving) the tags take the scrim laid over the colour they have (`utils/statusBarDim.ts`, from `useModalLayer`): once per open layer, so a confirmation over a sheet dims twice, as the page does; closing the last one gives every tag back what it had (a tag someone else repainted meanwhile is left). The scrim itself is `position: fixed; inset: 0` on `<body>`, covers the whole layout viewport with nothing transformed above it (`e2e/sheet-backdrop.spec.ts`). iOS and the browser tab take the same tag colour for their own chrome; no visible change there.
- **Geometry (#62, measured from an iOS 26 sheet on a 393 pt iPhone).** Grabber 36 × 5, 5 below the sheet's top edge. Title row 44 high, 16 below the top edge (title centre 38 down; iOS 26: 38), ending 60 down. 16 from the title row to the content, 16 under the content above the bottom inset (home indicator 34; Android gesture navigation 24, three-button 0: Material's 16 dp least). Side margins 20, as the screens. At its tallest a sheet stops 8 under the status bar (the inset, or `barTop` in a browser tab) and its content scrolls; otherwise it is as tall as its content (a fitted detent). iOS 26 also floats a partial-height sheet 8 pt off the sides and bottom with a 38 pt corner radius; Libellus keeps sheets attached with radius 30, which Material shares (28 dp).
- **Title row.** Cancel at the left, the action at the right, the title centred between them: both sides are as wide as the wider of the two, so a long title (a Collection's name) truncates with an ellipsis, centred, and never pushes Cancel aside. Once the sheet's content is scrolled, a hairline shows under the title row.
IDs: `<sheet>.sheetTitle` (the title), with `<sheet>`, `<sheet>.cancel`, `<sheet>.action`, `<sheet>.scrim` as before; confirmations `<dialog>`, `<dialog>.confirm`, `<dialog>.cancel`, `<dialog>.scrim`.
Flow: no Playwright flow (not a critical path); on the Android emulator `web/e2e/android/back.ts` (the owner's case: one book page to another, Edit read, the back gesture); the keyboard geometry in `web/tests/keyboard.test.ts`. The keyboard behaviour and the keyboard rising in the opening tap need a real device (not reproducible in Playwright): Android in `web/e2e/android/smoke.ts` (the Finish sheet's review field and the name sheet with Gboard up, docs/TESTING.md); iOS by hand.
Native: the platform's sheets and alerts do all of this themselves (`.sheet`, `presentationDetents`, `.alert`; `@FocusState` for the field). Android: a `ModalBottomSheet` / dialog consumes Back itself (`BackHandler`), and navigating away dismisses it.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Error codes of the access flow
The data layer reports stable codes, never sentences (`app/data/auth.ts`, `AuthErrorCode`); the copy lives under `auth.error.<code>`: `invite_required`, `invite_invalid`, `invite_expired`, `invite_exhausted` (Sign up), `invite_gone` (Verify), `code_invalid` (Verify), `rate_limited` (resend), `email_invalid`, `not_configured`, `unknown`.

### Search  (web: `components/search/Results.vue`, `components/search/ResultRow.vue`, `stores/search.ts`, `data/search.ts`, `data/merge.ts`, `data/apple.ts`, `data/openLibrary.ts`, `data/catalogueSearch.ts`; database: `search_books`)
Purpose: find a Book from anywhere without leaving the page: the content of the search overlay (Tab shell) above its query row.
States: idle (query shorter than 2 characters: `search.empty`) · loading (nothing has answered with a result yet, shown only after the typing pause and a little more: a book riffling its pages, `search.loadingShelves` under it, and a lamp hairline sweeping left to right above the query; the same words are read once for assistive tech, the state is busy; MOTION.md, Search loading) · results (best match at the bottom next to the query, weaker ones above it, the far end fading out; `search.hint` at the top, except for an ISBN query) · results growing while slower sources answer (no indicator of its own) · results dimmed while a newer query is on its way · no results (every source answered, none found anything: `search.noResultsTitle`, `search.noResults` with the query, `search.noResultsHint`, and the way to the manual-book sheet: `search.addManually`) · failed (every source failed: `search.failedTitle`, `search.failed`) · offline (#15: her own Library answers, see Offline and install; results with `search.offlineNote` where the hint would be, or `search.noResultsTitle` and `search.offlineNoResults` with the query and the note, never the way to add one by hand)
Layout: the results fill the palette from the query row upwards, the best match at the bottom, nearest to the thumb; each row is the cover, the serif title (two lines before it is cut, so a long title stays readable next to the Status), the authors with the year in mono, and at the right the + (or the Status once the Book is in the Library). The idle, loading, no-results and failed states take the same space, centred.
States: idle (query shorter than 2 characters: `search.empty`) · loading (nothing has answered with a result yet, shown only after the typing pause and a little more: a book riffling its pages, `search.loadingShelves` under it, and a lamp hairline sweeping left to right above the query; the same words are read once for assistive tech, the state is busy; MOTION.md, Search loading) · results (best match at the bottom next to the query, weaker ones above it, the far end fading out; `search.hint` at the top, except for an ISBN query) · results growing while slower sources answer (no indicator of its own) · results dimmed while a newer query is on its way · no results (every source answered, none found anything: `search.noResultsTitle`, `search.noResults` with the query, `search.noResultsHint`, and the way to the manual-book sheet: `search.addManually`) · failed (every source failed, or the device is offline: `search.failedTitle`, `search.failed`)
Actions → result:
- Type → after a 220 ms pause, and only from 2 characters, the query goes out to **three sources at once** (`createSearch().search`): the own Catalogue (`search_books`: the beginnings of the words of title and authors, accents and case ignored, `ß` as `ss`; the member's own Manual books included, other members' never), Apple Books (iTunes Search API, ebooks, 20 per storefront, two storefronts) and OpenLibrary (`search.json` with a reduced field list and the device language, 20 works, each as its best edition). Every answer is merged into the list as it arrives. Every new keystroke aborts the query in flight in every source; an answer only lands for the query it was asked for, so an outdated answer never overwrites a newer one. Clearing or shortening the query below 2 characters goes back to idle.
- An ISBN (10 or 13 digits, hyphens and spaces allowed, ISBN-10 converted) is looked up as an ISBN in every source (Catalogue by `isbn13`, Apple `lookup?isbn=`, OpenLibrary `search.json?isbn=`) instead of searched as text.
- Merging (`data/merge.ts`, `mergeResults`): Catalogue hits first, then Apple, then OpenLibrary, each in its own order. **The same edition** (Catalogue id, ISBN-13 with ISBN-10s converted, Apple track id, OpenLibrary edition key) is kept once; the first keeps its place and the later one fills its empty fields. **The same book** (normalised title + normalised first author: accents, case and punctuation ignored) is kept once too — one row per book, not per edition (owner decision on #12): the row shows the edition the member has, else the Catalogue's, else the first one found with a cover on Apple's CDN, else the first with any cover, else the first one found (the device language's storefront answers first; #63: a row without a cover is a Placeholder, and Apple's CDN answers in one round trip where OpenLibrary's takes up to three). A specific edition is one ISBN search away (`search.hint`).
- Ranking: by how well the query matches (`matchQuality`, 0–100): title = query (100) › title without its subtitle = query (92) › title and first-author words together, e.g. "piranesi clarke" (88) › first author = query (85) › title begins with the query (80, or 75 mid-word) › every word begins a word of the first author (70) › of the title (65) › of title and authors together (55) › of a later author only (45) › some words (up to 40). Ties: a book shown with a cover before one shown as a Placeholder (#63; mostly self-published papers OpenLibrary has no image of), then the Catalogue's, then popularity (Apple's rating count, OpenLibrary's reading-log and rating counts), then the order the sources gave. So "Piranesi" puts Susanna Clarke's novel next to the query and Gibbon's histories (Piranesi a later author) after every title match. **The match quality is then adjusted** (`rankScore`, #47): a book the member has in her Library, or another edition of one, whose title and authors hold every word of the query gets +50, so "dispossessed" finds hers next to the query although unrelated books carry the exact title; a match of title and first-author words ("piranesi clarke") gets +15, above an exact title alone; a summary or study guide of another book (`isSummary`: the words summary, study guide, SparkNotes, CliffsNotes, book review, key takeaways, chapter by chapter in the title) gets −60 unless she has it or the query asks for one. **A result shown without a cover (a Placeholder) also drops 10** (#104, `RANK.coverless`; every neighbouring match tier is within 10), so it never outranks a comparable one that has a cover, and a covered result of the next tier down comes first — while a much worse covered match still ranks below it. Exempt, so they stay on top: exact matches (the title itself, the title without its subtitle, title and author) and the member's own books (the +50 above), cover or not. The adjusted score decides; ties as above.
- **Titles and authors are text, not HTML** (#47): Apple's `trackName` and `artistName` go through `decodeEntities` (`data/entities.ts`; named entities such as `&ldquo;` `&amp;` `&auml;`, decimal and hexadecimal ones, decoded once; anything else stays as it was) before they are split into authors and stored, so "Klara y el Sol &ldquo;Klara and the Sun&rdquo;" reads with real quotes. Rows already in the Catalogue were cleaned by a migration (`20261003144500_decode_catalogue_entities.sql`, the same names in SQL, Catalogue Books only, a no-op where there is no entity).
- **Where a result comes from is never shown** (owner decision on #6): no source names, badges, counts or per-source loading. Each row: cover (`UiCover` sm: Apple's `120x180bb`, OpenLibrary's medium `-M` image; the first six, the ones above the keyboard, are preloaded when the answer lands and asked for at high priority, the rest load once they come within a list height of the list's view, watched against the list itself because its fade (a mask) stops the browser's own lazy loading from looking ahead (#63, docs/covers.md); an image that fails or loads smaller than 16 px on a side (a blank stand-in) falls back to OpenLibrary's cover of the same ISBN, then the Placeholder; a Catalogue Book brings its stored thumbhash and colours, so it sits on its blur while loading, others on a quiet fill; no image: the Placeholder cover), title (serif), authors, year (mono). A Book the member has (matched by Catalogue id, ISBN-13 or source id against her whole Library, read when the search opens) shows `status.<status>` with a check instead of the +. A row of a book she has **in another edition** (same title and first author, not the same Book) keeps its + and says `search.otherEdition` next to the year (`search.resultOtherEdition`).
- Tap a row → the book page `/book/<key>` (a Catalogue Book by id, otherwise `apple-<trackId>`, `ol-<edition key>` or `isbn-<isbn13>`); the route change closes the search. The touch-down already asks for the page's data (`UiPressLink`, `book.prefetch`); a mouse press navigates on press, a finger on its tap.
- Tap + (`search.add`, label `search.add` with the title) → the Add sheet over the search (Want to read is what it starts on; there is no one-tap add, the Status and its dates are chosen there). **Add does not wait for the network** (optimistic, see the Add sheet and Save offline and sync later): the sheet closes, the row shows its Status and any other row of the same book says `search.otherEdition` at once, the entry is on Want to read in the Library, and the add is sent in the background. If it is then refused the row is a + again, the other rows are what they were and the entry leaves the Library (the sync chip says *Couldn't sync*). While linking (below) the add is waited for as before: the file is linked to the entry's database id.
- **Her own Books first** (#131, `libraryGroup` in `data/search.ts`, `search.own` in `stores/search.ts`): the member's Library entries the query is about are a small group at the front of the list, next to the query, under the eyebrow *In your Library* (`search.inLibrary`), each with its cover, title, author and Status; the results the sources found follow without them, a hairline above. An entry is in the group when it matches the query as ranking reads it (`matchQuality` above `someWords`; an ISBN by her Book's ISBN-13), or when a result is *another edition* of it (the sources found the book by words her edition lacks: another language's title, a subtitle), or — opened by Find book — when its first author's surname is a word of the query (the file's author: a translated title still finds her edition). Best match first, then the other-edition ones, then by author, each in the Library's order. The entries are the Library as read when the palette opened (an entry added on another device counts), else as the device has it; the group shows at once, before any source answers. A tap on a group row opens the book page, as any row.
- **Finding an ebook's Book** (`search.linking`, opened by *Find book* on the Ebooks page; ends when the palette closes): a row is a button (`search.linkTo`, `search.linkOtherEdition`, `search.addAndLink` as its name), not a link. Her entry (in the group, or a result that is hers, whatever its Status) → the file is linked to it at once, the palette closes and the Ebooks page offers Undo; an entry that has an ebook already asks first (*<title> already has an ebook*: Replace / Keep, see Ebooks). A result marked *Other edition in your Library* (row or +) → the sheet **Which edition?** (see Ebooks): never a second entry for the same book. A new Book (row or +) → the Add sheet; once added, it is linked to the file.
Pointer: the row fills (`fill`) under a pointer that can hover (`@media (hover: hover)`), and presses to `fill-strong`; touch has no hover state.
Edge cases: Apple's storefronts follow the device language: German (`de`, `de-*`) → `de` then `us`; anything else → `us` then `gb`. **A failing source is silent**: the others' results stay, with no note; one storefront failing leaves the other's. Only when every source fails does the failed state show. Offline no source is asked at all: the member's own Library answers at once (Offline and install); when the connection comes back (or goes) with a query showing, it is asked again. The Library read failing only means nothing is marked. The list may grow up to the height above the query row (the visual viewport, so it stays above the keyboard) and scrolls; dragging inside the list scrolls rather than swiping the palette away. Closing the search drops the query and the results.
Copy keys: `search.addManually`, `search.empty`, `search.loadingShelves`, `search.hint`, `search.add`, `search.otherEdition`, `search.inLibrary`, `search.linkTo`, `search.linkOtherEdition`, `search.addAndLink`, `search.noResultsTitle`, `search.noResults`, `search.noResultsHint`, `search.failedTitle`, `search.failed`, `search.offlineNote`, `search.offlineNoResults`, `status.*`, `common.etAl`
IDs: `search.addManually`, `search.empty`, `search.loading`, `search.results`, `search.own`, `search.ownLabel`, `search.result`, `search.resultTitle`, `search.resultStatus`, `search.resultOtherEdition`, `search.add`, `search.hint`, `search.noResults`, `search.failed`, `search.offline`
Flow: `web/tests/cover-src.test.ts` (sizes per cover, fallbacks, blank detection, a cover that 404s or comes back blank), `web/tests/search.test.ts` (one list from every source, an ISBN looked up, a failing source, every source failing, her own books first) and `web/e2e/search-and-add.spec.ts` (search, open, add); Apple and OpenLibrary answer from the recordings in `web/tests/fixtures/apple` and `web/tests/fixtures/openlibrary`. The repository and the merge in `web/tests/search.test.ts` (merge order, dedupe, the edition with a cover shown, a covered result first among ties, storefront choice, ranking, other edition, stale queries, failing sources, her own group: by query, by another edition, by the file's author); linking from search in `web/e2e/ebooks.spec.ts`, the Catalogue against the stack in `web/tests/catalogue.test.ts`, the database function in `supabase/tests/catalogue_search_test.sql`
Native: the merge and ranking are pure functions (`data/merge.ts`) to port line for line; the Catalogue is the same `search_books` call.

### Book  (web: `app/pages/book/[key].vue`, `stores/book.ts`, `stores/reading.ts`)
Purpose: one Book: what it is, and the one action its state asks for.
Layout: a pushed screen: no tab header; the round back button (and ⋯), pinned under the safe area while the page scrolls under it, over the cover's light (`UiAmbient` in the cover's colours; while the cover is the Placeholder, in the cloth's colour, and the cover's own glow too), the cover large with its glow, title (serif, at most three lines: a longer one ends in an ellipsis and a tap on it shows all of it, another tap folds it again; the full title is also its `title` attribute), authors, the facts in mono eyebrow type (year · pages · publisher, whichever are known), a status line, the action, then About, then the **sections** below it (next: *More from the author*; see Book page sections). The tab bar and search stay available. **A cover in view stays** (every cover): once its image has loaded, a re-synced cover, another artwork of the same edition or a fallback never replaces it unless that image fails; another size of the same artwork is fine; changing the edition (another ISBN) is the deliberate change and swaps it (`UiCover` `identity`, `utils/cover.ts` `CoverShowing`, `tests/cover-showing.test.ts`).
The action area (owner, the calmer book page): **one action per state**, a full-width pill (`UiButton` `lg`, `block`) right under the status line (under the progress block while reading), always in the same place, so the eye finds it without reading. The lit (`primary`) pill is the one thing the state asks for; a Book with its ebook here adds at most one `quiet` pill under it. Ending a read (Finish, DNF) is not on the page: both are in the Update progress sheet, where the evening a book ends is recorded anyway. How she read it (Read as) is in the options sheet only. Per state: not in the Library → **Add to Library** (lit) · Want to read → **Start reading** (lit; with the ebook: **Read now** lit, Start reading quiet under it) · Currently reading → **Update progress** (lit, bookmark icon, opens the sheet; with the ebook: **Read now** lit, Update progress quiet under it) · Finished → **Read again** (quiet: nothing is asked of a finished Book) · DNF → **Start again** (quiet).
States: loading (no Book known yet: a cover-shaped placeholder) · not in the Library (`book.notInLibrary`, primary **Add to Library**) · Want to read (status line `status.want_to_read` · `book.addedOn` with the day; primary **Start reading**) · Currently reading (status line: a lit lamp dot, `status.reading` · `book.since` "Since 3 Oct · day 4", day 1 being the start day; the progress block, its bar and its figures 8 apart, 16 under the status line and 20 above the action; primary **Update progress** (`book.updateProgress`), which holds Finish and DNF) · Finished (status line: the latest finished session's Rating as stars with its value, if rated, `status.finished` · the end day; the quiet action **Read again**) · DNF (the latest session was abandoned: status line a slash icon, `status.notFinished` · the day it was abandoned; the quiet action **Start again**) · missing (`book.missingTitle`, `book.missing`) · error (`book.errorTitle`, `book.error`, Retry)
Actions → result:
- Add to Library (`book.add`) → the Add sheet. After the add the page shows the Status without reloading.
- Start reading (`book.start`) → the Start sheet. After the start the page shows Currently reading and Update progress, without reloading.
- Update progress (`book.updateProgress`, Currently reading) → the Update progress sheet (see *Reading progress*). Its **Finish** (`progress.finish`) → the Finish sheet; after the finish the page shows Finished with the Rating. Its **DNF** (`progress.abandon`) → the DNF sheet; after it the page shows DNF and Start again. Both save the wheel first (nothing is sent when it was not moved).
- Read again (`book.readAgain`, Finished) and Start again (`book.startAgain`, DNF) → the Start sheet in its "again" words (see Start sheet). After it the page shows Currently reading and Update progress, without reloading.
- More (`book.more`, only for a long description) → the whole description; it starts clamped to five lines.
- Back (`book.back`) → the previous page with its scroll position (the tab pages are kept alive); a page opened from a link with no history goes to Library.
- ⋯ (`book.options`, on every Book page) → the options sheet: Read as (see *Read as*), Share, Hide, Change edition, Series, the ebook, Remove from Library (see Remove from Library) for a Book in the Library; a Book that is not in the Library has **Change edition** only (see Change edition, *A Book not in the Library*). The reading history (Reading history, Edit read sheet) sits under the Collections row.
Edge cases: `/book/<uuid>` is a Catalogue Book (or the member's Manual book): the Book and the member's entry are read. `/book/apple-<id>` (and `/book/isbn-<isbn13>`) is a search result: shown at once from what search found, then checked against the Catalogue (by ISBN-13 first, then the source id, the same order `add_to_library` matches in); if it is there, the Catalogue Book and the member's entry replace it. Opened cold (a link, a reload), the source is asked again (Apple lookup in the device's two storefronts; `/book/ol-<key>` asks OpenLibrary for that edition; `/book/isbn-<isbn13>` asks Apple, then OpenLibrary); a Book no source has is missing, no storefront answering is the error state with Retry. The source of a Book is never shown. A refresh that fails keeps what is showing.
Copy keys: `book.back`, `book.pages`, `book.add`, `book.start`, `book.progress.update`, `book.read`, `book.readAgain`, `book.startAgain`, `book.since`, `book.notInLibrary`, `book.addedOn`, `rating.label`, `book.about`, `book.more`, `book.loading`, `book.missingTitle`, `book.missing`, `book.errorTitle`, `book.error`, `book.retry`, `status.*`, `common.dayMonth`, `common.etAl`
IDs: `book.back`, `book.hero`, `book.title`, `book.authors`, `book.facts`, `book.notInLibrary`, `book.actions`, `book.state`, `book.status`, `book.since`, `book.rating`, `book.add`, `book.start`, `book.updateProgress`, `book.read`, `book.readAgain`, `book.startAgain`, `book.about`, `book.description`, `book.more`, `book.loading`, `book.missing`, `book.retry`
Flow: `web/e2e/search-and-add.spec.ts` (open from search, add, back; an unknown link: `web/tests/search.test.ts`), `web/e2e/start-and-finish.spec.ts` (Start reading → one action, Update progress, with Finish and DNF in its sheet and no Read as on the page → Finish → Finished with its Rating), `web/tests/abandon.test.ts` (DNF with and without a reason, Start again, Read again), `web/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` (the Book in each status with its Update progress, Finish, Start and Add sheets, in the dark theme; the keyboard: Finish then DNF in the sheet's focus order, and focus back on Update progress)
Native: a pushed view in the navigation stack.

### Push to a book and back  (web: `composables/useBookFlight.ts`, `components/shell/BookFlight.vue` in `app/layouts/tabs.vue`, `utils/flight.ts`, `utils/snapshot.ts`, the tap in `components/ui/PressLink.vue`, `data-cover` on the sheet in `components/ui/Cover.vue`)
Purpose: the motion between a list and a book page (docs/MOTION.md, Push to a book): the tapped cover flies into the book page's hero and back into its row.
Layout: no screen of its own: two empty fixed layers in the shell (`shell.flightPage` under the chrome: a still copy of the page being left; `shell.flight` over everything but sheets: the flying cover, `shell.flightCover`). Both take no taps and are hidden from assistive technology; while a flight runs the top one carries `data-moving`.
States: at rest (layers empty) · pushing (cover flying in, book page fading in and rising `md`, the page left fading out; `standard`) · popping (cover flying back, book page fading out and sinking, the list fading in; `exit`, standard curve) · holding (landed with the hero's image in the air, the copy that flew stays on the hero, in its sheet, scrolling with the page, until the hero's own image is decoded and has faded in; `shell.flightHeld`) · waiting (landed on a book page whose Book is still loading: the copy stays on the page's stand-in, `data-flight-stand-in` in `book.loading`, at the hero cover's place, until the hero is drawn there and its image is in; `shell.flightHeld`) · Reduce Motion (cross-fade only, `standard`, both ways)
Actions → result:
- Tap a book's cover or title where its cover shows (Home reading card, Want to read, Next in your series, Library row or card, search result, Collection row, an author's work) → the book page as before (same address, same data on touch-down), with the cover flying from the tapped spot into the hero. A title tapped beside its cover flies the nearest cover of that Book on screen; one with no cover of its Book on screen gets the cross-fade, never a cover from off screen. The touch-down also starts loading the hero's image (the book page's size, `coverSrc(…, 'xl')`, from the tapped cover's address). In the air the row's image (sized for its row, 120 × 180) shows only while it is still sharp: the hero's image is laid over it as soon as it is decoded (at once when it is in, else faded in over `quick` when it arrives); until then the row's image fades to the thumbhash under it as the cover grows past its own pixels at the screen's density (× 1.15). So the cover lands sharp, or on its thumbhash, and never as the small image blown up. A Placeholder cover flies as cloth. The tab bar stays where it is.
- Back (`book.back`, the browser's back) from a book page opened that way → the page it came from, at exactly its saved scroll (and tab place), the cover flying back into the cover it was tapped on, even where the same Book shows twice on that page (Home: Want to read and Next in your series). Its row scrolled out of view, a book opened from search, or a list without its row → a cross-fade, no flight. A Back the browser animated itself (`hasUAVisualTransition`, iOS Safari's edge swipe) → no flight. Leaving the book page by a tab or a link → no motion.
- Back while the cover is still flying in → it turns around from where it is on screen; the same book tapped again while it flies back → it goes in again from there. Until the turned-around flight starts, the page being left stands in as a still copy at the strength the screen showed it, so the live page the router has already swapped in never shows at the other page's strength with its own cover beside the one in the air. Any other navigation mid-flight lands the flight at once.
Edge cases: a book page whose Book is still loading (one this device has not seen: an author's work, the next of a series, a link) shows a stand-in at the hero cover's place: the cover flies there, lands and waits in it (with the hero's image if it came, else its thumbhash), and the hero takes over once drawn in its place; the Book turning out missing (`book.missing`) or failing lets the copy go in a fade over `quick`. A page with neither hero nor stand-in (a public Book card still loading) → the cover waits for the hero up to `standard`, then fades where it is. From the tap (or Back) until the flight starts, a still copy of the page being left stands in for the live page (`data-flight-pose` on the root hides it), so the frame in which the router has drawn the new page but not resolved its scroll never shows it bare; after 1 s without the new page the live page shows again. A flight's animations hold still on their first frame and run from the next, so a slow first frame (Safari on iOS animates in another process) never shows the cover half way there. A cover's image fades in only once decoded (`UiCover`), its halo with it, cross-fading with the pool of colour; a cover that lands before the hero's image is in leaves nothing on the hero, which shows its thumbhash (or colour) and fades its own image in there. Motion tokens are read in either unit (`utils/motion.ts`, `parseDuration`): the built stylesheet writes them in seconds (`.25s`), the source in milliseconds. Scroll restoration is the router's (`app/router.options.ts`): the flight applies the router's chosen place one step early and measures there. Import's done-screen covers are not links, so nothing flies there.
IDs: `shell.flightPage`, `shell.flight`, `shell.flightCover`, `shell.flightHeld`
Flow: no flow (motion; frames by eye and on the emulator): `web/tests/flight.test.ts` (the FLIP transform, on-screen, where a turned-around flight starts), `web/tests/motion.test.ts` (tokens in `ms` and `s`), `web/e2e/android/flight.ts` (on the Android emulator: frame strips of the real tap, by network speed)
Native: the platform's zoom transition (iOS 18 `navigationTransition(.zoom(sourceID:in:))` with `matchedTransitionSource` on the cover; Android shared-element transition), interruptible by the back gesture.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Tab bar away  (web: `composables/useHideOnScroll.ts`, `utils/hideOnScroll.ts`, `components/shell/TabBar.vue`)
Purpose: on every page long enough for it (tab roots included) the tab bar steps out of the way while the member reads down and is back the moment she turns around (#82, docs/MOTION.md, Tab bar away); as iOS 26's `tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` and Material 3's navigation bar do. A page only a little longer than the screen keeps it (#205): it scrolls less than `MIN_END_PX` (640, about one screen) past the fold, so there is almost nothing below to read and the bar would go after a few pixels and have to come back before she is done.
Layout: no screen of its own: the tab bar (`shell.tabs`) slides down past the screen edge and fades out, and comes back the same way; nothing else moves (the page keeps its room under the bar, `clear-tab-bar`). While away the bar carries `data-away` and takes no taps.
States: shown · away · held (shown and not reading the scroll: search open or morphing, a sheet or confirmation on screen, a field with the keyboard, the router still putting a page in its place) · Reduce Motion (the bar never hides: no sliding and no popping in and out; a screen reader or keyboard reaching it brings it back, below)
Actions → result:
- Scroll down on a page that scrolls at least `MIN_END_PX` past the fold (Home, Library, the Profile, a book page, Collections, a Collection, Import, once their lists make them that long) → once the page has gone 10 px (`INTENT_PX`) down from where the scroll last turned, the bar slides away (`exit`). Scroll up 10 px from the turning point → it slides back (`standard`). Movements shorter than that, back and forth, change nothing. A page under `MIN_END_PX` of scroll keeps the bar wherever she is on it; content that later grows the page past the threshold hides it on the next scroll down, and one that shrinks under it brings it back.
- At the top of the page (or above it, iOS's rubber band) and within 10 px of its end → shown, whatever the direction.
- Search opened while the bar is away (a page's search prompt) → the bar is in its resting place at once, without a transition, and the palette grows out of it; after search closes it is showing.
- A sheet or a confirmation opens → the bar is back (under the scrim) and stays until it has gone; the page then counts afresh.
- Focus in a field that brings up the keyboard, or keyboard focus moving into the bar (a screen reader's focus moving onto it too: the web has no reliable signal that one is running, so reachability is by focus) → shown.
- Nothing is drawn behind the bar: no fade and no scroll edge (iOS 26 applies its automatic soft scroll edge under a tab bar, and the app opts out: the capsule's own glass, edge and shadow carry it, and it slides away on scroll down anyway). Native: iOS — hide the bottom scroll edge effect (`scrollEdgeEffectStyle(.hidden, for: .bottom)`) if the system draws one that reads heavy; Android — nothing to add.
- Any change of page (a link, a tab, Back from a book page with the bar away) → shown on the page it lands on; the cover flight runs as before.
Edge cases: a scroll the member did not make is not read as intent: while the document carries `data-moving` (the router restoring a place) only the turning point follows it, as for any jump longer than a screen in one event. The page's end is measured against the taller of `innerHeight` and the layout viewport, so Chrome on Android, whose toolbar collapses as the page scrolls, still shows the bar at the very end.
IDs: `shell.tabs` (`data-away` while away)
Flow: no flow (motion): `web/tests/hide-on-scroll.test.ts` (direction, threshold, turning points, top and end, jumps, short pages)
Native: iOS — `UITabBarController`'s `tabBarMinimizeBehavior` / SwiftUI `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` on the tab bar; Android — a `HideBottomViewOnScrollBehavior` (or Compose `BottomAppBarScrollBehavior`) on every destination.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Add sheet  (web: `components/book/AddSheet.vue`, `components/book/AddStatusFields.vue`, `stores/library.ts`)
Purpose: put a Book into the Library with a Status (D's add-sheet).
Layout: `UiSheet` (Cancel at the top left, title `add.title`), the Book (`UiBookLine`), the status part (below), the error if any, the primary **Add to Library** at the bottom.
States: choosing · busy (`add.busy`, button disabled) · error (`library.error.<code>` under the status part; the sheet stays open; the error goes away when anything is changed)
Actions → result:
- Add to Library (`add.submit`), **opened from a search result's + (optimistic)** → nothing waits for the network: the days are checked first (`checkAddDraft`), then the add goes into the outbox as `add_to_library` (the same line, order, retries and refusal handling as an offline write, see Save offline and sync later) and the entry is shown as it will be: the sheet closes, the entry is in its Status's list, the result's mark flips, Home and the book page show it. The entry's id is the device's own (`local-…`) and its Book's id is the **page key** (`apple-…`, `ol-…`, `isbn-…`), so every link to it opens its page (`/book/apple-…`) and nothing navigates with a temporary id; once the database answers, the Library is read again and the entry is the database's (a Catalogue id), under the same page key. A second tap on the same Book while it waits is refused on the device (`already_in_library`, in the sheet), so it is added once. The Cover (below) is resolved by the outbox just before the first send, never waited for; a result's own cover shows meanwhile. A refusal (`already_in_library` from another device, `book_invalid`, …) leaves the line as a failure (`sync.failure`, the chip *Couldn't sync*, said once and dismissable), the entry and the result's mark go away on the Library's next read (`reconcile` in `stores/library.ts`), and the other editions of the Book are no longer marked. Opened from the book page, an author's page or while linking an ebook (the file is linked to the entry's database id), the add waits for the database as it always did:
- Add to Library (`add.submit`) otherwise → if the Book is not in the Catalogue yet, its Cover is resolved first (`resolveBookCover`, at most 6 s), trying in order until an image will do: the Apple artwork → Apple's edition with the Book's ISBN → OpenLibrary's cover by its cover id → OpenLibrary's cover by ISBN. Each is read (fetched with CORS, decoded off the page) from a smaller copy; one shorter than 150 px on its short side, or of one flat colour, is passed over. The one that will do is kept large (Apple `…/600x900bb.jpg`, OpenLibrary `-L.jpg`) with its thumbhash and two colours; if none will do the Book gets the Placeholder cover (no URL), unless its own image only could not be read in time (then it keeps that URL without thumbhash and colours). The days are checked first, in the member's own today (`checkAddDraft`), before the cover is looked up or anything is sent. Then **one database call**, `add_to_library(p_book, p_status, p_started_on, p_ended_on, p_rating, p_review)`: finds the Catalogue Book by ISBN-13 or source id (or adds this snapshot, as the large `…/600x900bb.jpg` artwork URL with the thumbhash and colours; the first snapshot is kept), creates the entry and, for Currently reading and Finished, its first Reading session, all in the one call (a refusal leaves nothing behind, not even a new Catalogue Book). The sheet closes; Library, the book page and search show the entry at once.
- Cancel, the scrim, a swipe down, Escape → closes, nothing is added.
Edge cases: the Statuses offered are `ADDABLE_STATUSES` (Want to read, Currently reading, Finished). Adding a Book the member already has fails with `already_in_library`, whatever the Status, and changes nothing. The sheet opens on Want to read with no dates, every time.
Copy keys: `add.title`, the status part's, `add.action`, `add.busy`, `library.error.*`, `common.cancel`
IDs: `add`, `add.cancel`, `add.scrim`, the status part's with `add` as the prefix, `add.error`, `add.submit`
Flow: `web/e2e/search-and-add.spec.ts` (from the book page and from a result's +), `web/tests/optimistic-add.test.ts` (the optimistic add from a result's +: shown at once, refused → undone) and `web/tests/add-with-status.test.ts` (a past read as Finished with dates, 4.25 stars and a review; Currently reading with a refused future day); the database rules in `supabase/tests/library_test.sql` and `supabase/tests/add_with_status_test.sql`, the repository in `web/tests/library.test.ts` and `web/tests/add-with-status.test.ts`
Native: a sheet.

### Status part of the Add sheets  (web: `components/book/AddStatusFields.vue`, `AddDraft` and its rules in `data/library.ts`)
Purpose: choose the Status a Book is added with and, for Currently reading and Finished, the first read, in the same sheet and the same call (D's add-sheet: status first, the dates follow the choice). Shared by the Add sheet (`add`) and the manual-book sheet (`manual`); the IDs below take the sheet's name as their prefix.
Layout: the eyebrow `add.statusLabel`, the Status as radio rows (the chosen one lit in the lamp colour; what it needs on the right: `add.needs.<status>`), then the choice's own part, faded in:
- *Want to read*: nothing (no dates, no Rating, no review).
- *Currently reading*: the eyebrow `add.datesLabel` and one date row, **Started on** (`add.startedOn`), today by default.
- *Finished*: the eyebrow `add.datesLabel` and two date rows, **Started on** (optional; `add.notSet` while empty; **Remove start date**, `add.clearStarted`, appears under the rows once it is set) and **Finished on** (`add.endedOn`, today by default); then the Rating control (the same as the Finish sheet's, optional, quarter stars) and the Review box (`add.review`, `add.optional`, `add.reviewPlaceholder`, at most 10,000 characters).
Actions → result:
- Choose a Status (`<prefix>.status.<status>`) → the draft takes that Status and the days it starts with (below); the part that belongs to it fades in.
- Date rows (`<prefix>.started`, `<prefix>.ended`) → the platform's date picker; **Remove start date** (`<prefix>.clearStarted`) empties the optional start of a Finished read.
- Rating (`<prefix>.rating`) and Review (`<prefix>.review`) → as in the Finish sheet; both optional.
States: the draft starts on Want to read with nothing chosen. Choosing a Status gives it the days it starts with (`chooseAddStatus`): Currently reading started today; Finished ended today with no start (a past read often has none). The Rating and review stay when the Status changes and come back if Finished is chosen again; days do not. A day the draft refuses (below) is shown in the error colour on its rows.
Rules (the database's, `add_first_session`, same as start and finish; the sheet checks days first and the database is the authority): Currently reading needs a start day; Finished needs an end day, may have a start day, a Rating of 1–20 quarters and a review (trimmed; blank is none); no day after the member's today (the database allows today anywhere on Earth); the end is not before the start; dates on Want to read, an end, Rating or review on Currently reading, a Rating or review on Want to read are `session_invalid`.
Date pickers: the platform's own (an invisible `<input type="date">` over the row); the start's maximum is the end, the end's minimum is the start, the maximum of both is today.
Copy keys: `add.statusLabel`, `add.needs.<status>`, `add.datesLabel`, `add.startedOn`, `add.notSet`, `add.clearStarted`, `add.endedOn`, `add.review`, `add.optional`, `add.reviewPlaceholder`, `status.*`, `rating.*`
IDs (prefix `add` or `manual`): `<prefix>.status.<want_to_read|reading|finished>` (radio), `<prefix>.reading` and `<prefix>.finished` (the part shown), `<prefix>.started`, `<prefix>.ended` (the date inputs), `<prefix>.clearStarted`, `<prefix>.rating` (with `.value`), `<prefix>.review`
Flow: no flow; `web/tests/add-with-status.test.ts`
Native: the platform's date pickers; the rest as above.

### Start sheet  (web: `components/book/StartSheet.vue`, `stores/reading.ts`)
Purpose: start the first read of a *Want to read* Book (issue #7; issue #1, story 31), or the next read of a closed one: Read again after a finished read, Start again after an abandoned one (issue #10; story 36). One sheet, one day row; `startKind` (`start` | `again` | `restart`) only changes the words and the database call.
Layout: `UiSheet` (Cancel at the top left, title `start.title`, or `start.titleAgain` / `start.titleRestart`), the Book (`UiBookLine`), one grouped row `start.startedOn` with the day in words (`UiDateRow`: "Today · 3 Oct", `common.today|yesterday`, other days `common.dayMonth` or `common.dayMonthYear`) and a chevron, the error if any, the primary **Start reading** (**Read again** / **Start again**, with the repeat icon) at the bottom.
States: choosing (the day defaults to the member's today, on the device's calendar) · busy (`start.busy`, button disabled) · error (`library.error.<code>` under the row, the day error-coloured for a day problem; the button reads `start.retry` and tries again)
Actions → result:
- The day row → the platform's own date picker (an invisible native date input over the row), latest day today.
- Start reading (`start.submit`) → the day is checked on the device (missing → `date_invalid`, after today → `date_in_future`); then **one database call**, `start_reading(p_entry_id, p_started_on)`, which opens a session; the entry's Status turns to Currently reading in the database. For a Book read before (`startKind` again / restart) the call is **`read_again(p_entry_id, p_started_on)`** instead: it opens a new session when the latest one is closed and keeps the earlier ones. The sheet closes, the book page and the Library show the new state without reloading.
- Cancel, the scrim, a swipe down, Escape → closes, nothing is started.
Edge cases: a second device starting the same Book first → `already_reading`; a Book read before, started as a first read → `already_finished` (the sheet calls `read_again` for it); `read_again` on a Book being read → `already_reading`, on one never read → `never_read`. The database refuses a day after today in every time zone (`date_in_future`), so a member east of UTC can always start "today".
Copy keys: `start.title`, `start.titleAgain`, `start.titleRestart`, `start.startedOn`, `start.action`, `start.actionAgain`, `start.actionRestart`, `start.busy`, `start.retry`, `common.today`, `common.yesterday`, `common.dayMonth`, `common.dayMonthYear`, `common.chooseDay`, `library.error.*`, `common.cancel`
IDs: `start`, `start.cancel`, `start.scrim`, `start.date`, `start.error`, `start.submit`
Flow: `web/e2e/start-and-finish.spec.ts` (start today, Finish); a day in the future refused and a failed call (`web/tests/reading.test.ts`), Start again and Read again (`web/tests/abandon.test.ts`); the rules in `supabase/tests/reading_sessions_test.sql` and `supabase/tests/abandon_and_read_again_test.sql`, the repository in `web/tests/reading.test.ts` and `web/tests/abandon.test.ts`
Native: a sheet; the day row opens the platform's date picker.
