### Your shelf  (web: `app/pages/profile/shelf.vue`, `components/shelf/{Stage,Row,RowCard,Pile,YearRow}.vue`, `components/profile/Shelf.vue`, `composables/useShelfPreload.ts`, `stores/shelf.ts`, `data/shelf.ts`, `utils/shelfOwner.ts`, the `immersive` page meta in `components/shell/TabBar.vue`; a Back off the row's own history entry in `composables/useBackDismiss.ts` (also over Home's *Read in* sheet, `components/home/TallySheet.vue`); the Regal layer in `nuxt.config.ts` + `regal.config.ts`)
Purpose: the owner's Library as Regal's 3D shelf (a row in a card, the full Stack), the same one the portfolio shows (issue #23): one account only, completely hidden for everyone else.
Who: the member whose auth user id is `NUXT_PUBLIC_SHELF_OWNER_ID` (`isShelfOwner`); none configured → nobody. For anyone else there is no card, no row, no request for the file or for Regal's code, and `/profile/shelf` is a page that doesn't exist (the route's `validate`, the same 404 as any unknown address). The file is public (the portfolio shows it), so this hides screens, it protects nothing; a native port reads the same setting.
Data: the published Regal library file (`runtimeConfig.public.regal.librarySrc` = `https://books.fabkho.dev/v2/library.json`, written from this app's data by `export:regal` and Regal assets): editions, dates, ratings and art exactly as on fabkho.dev/books. `data/shelf.ts` reads it with the vendored validator (`readShelf`): every Book newest read first (undated last), each with its Spine colour (the art's average, else the palette's background) and page count; a year's Books are those whose `dateRead` is in it (`booksReadIn`, as Regal's Stack filters a year). Regal's components read the file again themselves (the page's cache of the file, `preloadRegal`'s, or the browser's answers).
Layout — **Profile** (`ProfileShelf`, before the account rows): the eyebrow `shelf.card.title` with the count in ghost mono, a quiet **Show all ›** beside it only when the shelf holds more Books than the row (`SHELF_ROW_LIMIT`, 80), and under it a raised card (`ShelfRowCard`, 18rem tall) filled edge to edge by Regal's row (`RegalBooksRow`, `inspect="viewport"`) of the newest 80 Books, oldest at the left, the row starting at the newest at the right, a sheet and the month between months; before the file has come the section is there without its count. **Your shelf** (`/profile/shelf`): a pushed screen that fills the column, the night room in either theme (`data-theme="dark"` on the page; the status bar takes the room's colour while it is open), the round back button pinned with the title `shelf.title` and the count (`shelf.count`, or `shelf.yearCount` with a year) on its row, the tab bar away (`immersive`), and Regal's Stack filling the rest, its view reaching up behind the top bar so the pile starts above the middle. **Year in review** (owner only, under the months): the eyebrow `shelf.year.title` with the year's count and the same card with Regal's row of that year's Books (`year`), starting at January; no page of its own. The rows wear the app's theme, light or dark (`regal-themed`, `theme="auto"`), their details as Libellus' own, broken out on a phone a quieter cousin of the app's sheets (`ShelfBookSheet`: the grabber; a title row with a plain Done at the left, which puts the Book back, and Back cover / Front cover at the right in the lamp colour; then the title, one mono line of author · day · pages, the blurb in four lines, a trailing Goodreads link; the bottom inset cleared), where the shelf page's Stack keeps its pills.
Warming (owner only): on idle (`requestIdleCallback`, a timer where there is none) the signed-in shell, Home and the Profile call Regal's `preloadRegal` through a dynamic import (`useShelfPreload`): the library file, the `regal` chunk and the first Spines of the Profile's row (the newest 80), of the year Home's *Read in* sheet shows and of the year in view on the Profile (or the latest), one after the other. A card a screen away asks again (the same call, shared). Nobody else's session imports it or fetches the file or the chunk; the chunk stays out of the entry and the precache.
States: loading (the shelf page: the Books as slabs in their Spines' colours, `ShelfPile`, while Regal's Stack loads, the 3D fading in over it; the cards have no stand-in: the card keeps its size and is its plain surface, no slabs, no spinner, until the row has drawn the Spines in view, then Regal's intro plays; warmed ahead, the row shows its Spines on its first frame; the row mounts with its screen and Regal plays the intro when the card is first seen) · the row, the Stack · load error (`shelf.loadError`, **Try again**; in the Profile's card too) · offline before anything loaded (`shelf.offline`; tried again when the connection is back) · a file Regal can't show (`invalid`: the same error state). Regal's own: the details card (or the phone's sheet) of a Book that is out (its copy, English, Regal's), its caption of the Book in the middle, the row's ‹ › and month labels.
Actions → result:
- The row (Regal): swipe sideways to scroll it (the page still scrolls up and down), tap a Book → it breaks out: the Book comes to the middle of the whole screen over a paper veil, above the header and the tab bar (`--regal-row-z-index` 50), its details as the phone's sheet or a card, the page held; tap again → turn it over, drag → turn it freely; the sheet's Done (Regal's round Back is off), Escape, a drag of the sheet's grabber, a tap beside it, or the system's Back (Regal's own history entry, which `useBackDismiss` lets pass the router by, so the page never navigates) → it lands back in the row.
- **Show all** (only above the cap) → Your shelf. `/profile/shelf?year=<year>` still shows that year only, though nothing links there.
- In the Stack (Regal): scroll, drag or flick through the pile (it glides on); tap a Book → it comes out with its card; tap again → its back; drag → turn it; the card's Show back / Put back.
- Back (the system's) with a Book out → it goes back on the pile, the page stays (a layer, `useBackDismiss`); otherwise → where the shelf was opened from (by history), else the Profile or the year.
Edge cases: Regal, three.js and TresJS load only for the owner (the `regal` chunk, through `LazyShelfStage`, `LazyShelfRow` and the idle warm-up's dynamic import; a build fails if the entry ever imports it) and are never precached (`globIgnores`); once fetched they are kept (CacheFirst), the library file NetworkFirst, its images CacheFirst (500, purged first). The Stack view (sort, year, separators) is Regal's and kept in the address (`?year=2025&group=month`). `?year=` outside 1000–9999 is ignored (the whole Library).
Assistive tech: Regal's canvas names no Book, so the row and the Stack render Regal's own visually hidden Book list (`accessible-list`: one button per Book, "Title, Author, finished March 2025, 4 of 5 stars", which takes the Book out). A Book taken out is a `role="dialog"`, named "{title} details" (the sheet fills Regal's `#detail` slot), modal when the row breaks out. The shelf page's title in the top bar is its h1.
Copy keys: `shelf.title`, `shelf.back`, `shelf.count`, `shelf.yearCount`, `shelf.loading`, `shelf.loadError`, `shelf.offline`, `shelf.retry`, `shelf.card.title|rowLabel|all|allLabel`, `shelf.year.title|rowLabel`, `shelf.detail.done|backCover|frontCover|reading|goodreads`
IDs: `profile.shelf`, `profile.shelfCount`, `profile.shelfRow`, `profile.shelfAll`, `profile.shelfError`, `profile.shelfRetry`, `shelf`, `shelf.back`, `shelf.count`, `shelf.stage`, `shelf.loading`, `shelf.error`, `shelf.retry`, `yearInReview.shelf`, `yearInReview.shelfCount`, `yearInReview.shelfRow`, `shelfRow.sheet`, `shelfRow.title`, `shelfRow.detailMeta`, `shelfRow.about`, `shelfRow.flip`, `shelfRow.putBack`, `shelfRow.goodreads`
Flow: no Playwright flow (the owner's shelf is not a critical path); the library file the synthetic `web/tests/fixtures/shelf/library.json`; the data in `web/tests/shelf.test.ts`; on the Android emulator `web/e2e/android/shelf.ts` (real fingers: swipe the row, take a Book out, turn it, the system Back and Done put it away, the year's row).

### Error codes of the Library
`app/data/library.ts`, `LibraryErrorCode`; copy under `library.error.<code>`: `already_in_library` (the member already has this Book), `book_invalid` (no title, not an Apple Books or OpenLibrary snapshot, or nothing to find the Book by again), `status_unsupported` (a Status this version cannot add with yet), `not_signed_in`, `unknown` (also for `book_conflict`, a concurrent add of the same new Book that rolled back: trying again works). The database raises them as the messages of `add_to_library`. The session actions (#7) add `entry_not_found` (no such entry in the member's Library, another member's included), `already_reading` (start: a read is open), `already_finished` (start: read before; `read_again` starts the next read), `not_reading` (finish: no open read), `date_invalid` (no day), `date_in_future` (a day after today), `ended_before_started`, `rating_invalid` (outside 1–20 quarters), `review_too_long` (over 10,000 characters); a network failure is `unknown`. DNF and read again (#10) add `reason_too_long` (an abandon reason over 1,000 characters) and `never_read` (read again: the entry has no read yet; its first read is Start reading); `not_reading`, `already_reading`, `date_*` and `ended_before_started` are raised by `abandon_reading` and `read_again` as well. The sheets check the day themselves first, so the member hears why before anything is sent; the database is the authority.
`app/data/library.ts`, `LibraryErrorCode`; copy under `library.error.<code>`: `already_in_library` (the member already has this Book), `book_invalid` (no title, not an Apple Books or OpenLibrary snapshot, or nothing to find the Book by again), `session_invalid` (adding with days, a Rating or a review that do not belong to the Status; #9), `not_signed_in`, `unknown` (also for `book_conflict`, a concurrent add of the same new Book that rolled back: trying again works). The database raises them as the messages of `add_to_library`. The session actions (#7) add `entry_not_found` (no such entry in the member's Library, another member's included), `already_reading` (start: a read is open), `already_finished` (start: read before; Read again is #10), `not_reading` (finish: no open read), `date_invalid` (no day; also adding as Currently reading without a start or as Finished without an end), `date_in_future` (a day after today), `ended_before_started`, `rating_invalid` (outside 1–20 quarters), `review_too_long` (over 10,000 characters); a network failure is `unknown`. The sheets check the day themselves first, so the member hears why before anything is sent; the database is the authority.
Read as (#169) adds `read_as_invalid` (a way other than physical, ebook or audiobook).
Offline and install (#15) adds `offline`: the device had no connection, so the repository refused the write before anything was sent (`WriteOptions`); the manual-book call and the Collections share it.

### Manual book sheet  (web: `components/book/ManualSheet.vue`, `stores/manual.ts`, `data/manualBooks.ts`, the link in `components/search/Results.vue`)
Purpose: type a book in by hand when search finds nothing, and track it like any other (D's manual-book). The Book is the member's own: private to her Library, never in the Catalogue, never in anybody else's search. Her followers see it with her reads, unless she hides it (*Hide from followers*).
Layout: `UiSheet` (Cancel at the top left, title `manual.title`, **Add** at the right) over the search. The Placeholder cover as a preview (`UiCover` md, follows the title and author as they are typed; `manual.coverNote`), a group of two rows, Title\* and Author\*, a second group of two optional rows, ISBN and Pages (mono), the reason a field is wrong, the status part of the Add sheets (above) with the Statuses, the dates, Rating and review, the error of the call if any, the lock line `manual.private` ("It stays out of the shared catalogue. Your followers see it with your reads; hide it from them in its ⋯ menu."), the primary **Add to Library**. A row is label left, input right-aligned; the row being typed in is lit in the lamp tint.
Entry: in the search's no-results state, the quiet button `search.addManually` under `search.noResultsHint`. It opens the sheet with what was typed: an ISBN fills ISBN, anything else fills Title.
States: editing · busy (`manual.busy`, action and button disabled) · a field wrong (its label and text in the error colour, one line `manual.invalid.<field>` under the groups) · error (`manual.error.<code>`, or `library.error.<code>` for the days, Rating and review; under the status part; the sheet stays open and the error goes away when the status part changes)
Actions → result:
- Add (`manual.action`, `manual.submit`, or Enter in a field) → the fields are checked first (title and author not blank; ISBN 10 or 13 digits with a matching check digit, hyphens and spaces allowed; pages a whole number above zero) and nothing is sent while one is wrong; the days follow, as in the Add sheet. Then **one call**, `add_manual_book(title, authors, isbn, page_count, status, started_on, ended_on, rating, review)`: the Book (`source = manual`, `owner_id` = the member, an ISBN-10 also stored as ISBN-13), the member's entry with the chosen Status and, for Currently reading and Finished, its first Reading session; it returns the entry. The sheet closes, the search closes with its query, and the Book's page opens (`/book/<id>`); the Library lists it under its Status (a Finished one under the year it ended). The sheet opens on Want to read, as before.
- Cancel (`manual.cancel`), the scrim, Escape or a swipe down → the sheet closes; the search is as it was.
Placeholder cover: a Manual book has no image, so everywhere it appears (Library rows, the book page, the sheet) `UiCover` draws title and author on a cloth, picked by the title (`clothOf`, six cloths, the same in both themes). The same title always gets the same cloth. The type is clamped to the cover: the title in at most four lines at 12.5 % of the cover's width (up to 36 characters), six at 10.5 % (up to 70), eight at 8.5 % (longer), then an ellipsis; the author in at most two; never past the inset rule. Under `md` (the `xs` and `sm` covers, 30 and 40 pt) there is no type, only the cloth, the inset rule and the mark (the title stays the cover's accessible name). `UiCover` emits `fallback` while the Placeholder shows.
Edge cases: private by RLS, not by filtering: `books_readable` shows a row with an owner to its owner only, so another member cannot read it, find it by title or ISBN, add it to a Library or open its page (missing); the one exception is her followers, who see her Manual books with her reads (the feed, her profile, her figures; the Book is sent with `manual: true`, so its cover opens nothing), and members never write `books` directly (not even their own Manual books; editing them is not part of v1). Two members typing in the same book each get their own; the Catalogue's unique indexes leave Manual books out. Deleting a member deletes her Manual books. A refused call (a wrong ISBN, a day in the future) leaves no Book behind.
Copy keys: `search.addManually`, `manual.title`, `manual.action`, `manual.coverNote`, `manual.field.<title|author|isbn|pageCount>`, `manual.optional`, `manual.private`, `manual.submit`, `manual.busy`, `manual.invalid.<field>`, `manual.error.<code>`, the status part's, `common.cancel`
IDs: `search.addManually`, `manual`, `manual.scrim`, `manual.cancel`, `manual.action`, `manual.cover`, `manual.title`, `manual.author`, `manual.isbn`, `manual.pageCount`, `manual.invalid`, `manual.error`, the status part's with `manual` as the prefix, `manual.private`, `manual.submit`

### Error codes of the manual-book call
`app/data/manualBooks.ts`, `ManualBookErrorCode` = the Library's codes plus `isbn_invalid`; copy under `manual.error.<code>`. The database raises them as the messages of `add_manual_book`: `book_invalid` (no title, no author, or a page count that is not positive), `isbn_invalid` (not 10 or 13 digits, or the check digit does not add up), `not_signed_in`; `unknown` for the rest. ### Collections  (web: `app/pages/collections/index.vue`, `components/collections/{LibraryLink,Mosaic,NameSheet}.vue`, `stores/collections.ts`, `data/collections.ts`)
Purpose: the member's own shelves (Favourites, Sci-fi, Gifts…), reached from the Library tab (D's collections). Non-exclusive: a Book can be on any number of them.
Layout: in the Library tab, above the Status segments (and above an empty Library's empty state while there are Collections), a quiet rounded row (`library.collections`): a small fan of the first covers of the first three Collections, `collections.title`, how many Collections there are (mono), a chevron. It opens `/collections`, a pushed screen in the tab layout (the Library stays alive under it): the round back button and a round + (`collections.newTop`) in the top bar, the title `collections.title` large, `collections.intro`, then one row per Collection in her order — the mosaic (the covers of its first four Books, two by two, on a small raised card lit by the first cover; empty places drawn as hairline cover outlines), the name (serif), `collections.count` — and at the end a dashed mosaic-sized box with `collections.new`.
States: loading (nothing yet) · list · no Collections (the lamp over the empty shelf: `collections.emptyTitle`, `collections.empty`, the primary `collections.new`) · load error (`collections.loadError`, Retry)
Actions → result:
- Tap a Collection → its page `/collections/<id>`.
- `collections.new` or the + → the **name sheet** (`UiSheet`, title `collections.new`, the action **Create** at the top right, Enter does the same): one row, `collections.nameLabel` with the field (placeholder `collections.namePlaceholder`, at most 80 characters, focused in the tap that opens the sheet, so the keyboard comes up in it). **One call**, `create_collection(name)`: trimmed, inner runs of whitespace collapsed; the new Collection goes to the end of her list. The sheet closes and the row appears; a refusal stays in the sheet under the field (`collections.error.<code>`).
- Back (`collections.back`) → the Library, by history when it came from there.
Edge cases: a name she already uses, in any case, is `name_taken` (names are per member: someone else may have the same); blank or longer than 80 characters is `name_invalid` (Create stays disabled while the field is blank). The list and the Library row refresh whenever the Library shows again. Signing out (or another member signing in) forgets them.

### Collection  (web: `app/pages/collections/[id].vue`, `components/collections/EntryList.vue`, `stores/collections.ts`)
Purpose: one Collection's Books in her order, to read down like a reading list and to put in order (D's collection).
Layout: a pushed screen: the round back button and the round more button over the first cover's light (`UiAmbient`), the name large in the serif, `collection.count` (and `collection.order` from two Books on), then the rows: the place in mono (`01`, `02`…), a small cover, the title (serif), the author and the Status (`status.<status>`, Currently reading in the lamp colour), and a grip at the right.
States: loading (the name from the list while the Books arrive) · Books · empty (`collection.emptyTitle`, `collection.empty`, the search prompt) · missing (`collection.missingTitle`, `collection.missing`, a link to all Collections) · error (`collection.errorTitle`, `collection.error`, Retry) · a new order that could not be saved (`collection.reorderError` above the list, which shows the database's order again)
Actions → result:
- **Drag a row's grip** (mouse or finger, at once), or **hold a row still** with a finger for 350 ms (moving first scrolls; a quick tap opens the Book) → the row lifts (raised on the sheet surface, a little larger, its number in the lamp colour) and follows the finger 1:1; the others glide out of its way over `quick` and the numbers show where each will end up. Near the top or bottom of the screen the page scrolls along. Letting go glides it into its place and **one call**, `reorder_collection(id, [every entry id, first to last])`, saves the order; the screen does not wait for it. Moves are saved one after another, so the last order wins.
- **Keyboard**: the grip is a button (`collection.move` with the title; hint `collection.moveHint`). Arrow up / down move the Book one place, Home / End to the top / the end, saved the same way; the focus stays on the moved Book's grip and a polite live region says `collection.moved` (`collection.lifted` / `collection.dropped` for a drag).
- Tap a row → the book page (`UiPressLink`, started on touch-down).
- More (`collection.more`) → the options sheet (title: the name): **Rename** (`collection.rename`) → the name sheet with the name filled in, **Save** at the top right, `rename_collection(id, name)`, same refusals as creating; **Delete collection** (`collection.delete`, danger) → the same sheet asks `collection.deleteConfirm` with `collection.deleteKeeps` and the danger button; `delete_collection(id)` → back to the Collections list. Deleting never removes a Book from the Library.
- Back (`collection.back`) → where it came from.
Edge cases: the order is the database's (`collection_entries.position`); a list that no longer matches what is on the Collection (a Book added or taken off on another device since it was read) is refused as `order_mismatch` and the page reloads. Reduce Motion: nothing glides, the row still moves with the finger. A Collection deleted elsewhere is missing. Not one of hers (another member's id) is missing too: RLS shows nothing.

### Collection picker  (web: `components/collections/{BookRow,PickerSheet}.vue` in `app/pages/book/[key].vue`, `stores/collections.ts`)
Purpose: put the Book of a book page on Collections or take it off (D's book detail, its Collections).
Layout: on the book page, between the action and About, the eyebrow `collections.title`, the Collections the Book is on as chips (each opens its Collection; one line, a long name ends in an ellipsis inside the chip and its `title` is the full name), then the outlined chip **Add to collection** (`collections.addTo`). It opens the picker: `UiSheet` (Cancel at the top left, title `collections.pickerTitle`, **Done** at the right), the Book (`UiBookLine`), for a Book not in the Library yet the line `collections.addsToLibrary`, then every Collection as a row — its first cover (or an outline), its name, `collections.count` — with a lamp check on the ones the Book is on; under them **New collection**.
States: choosing · a row busy (disabled while its call is on its way) · error (`collections.error.<code>` under the rows; the sheet stays open)
Actions → result:
- Tap a Collection without the check (the rows wait until the Book's Collections are known) → **one call**, `add_to_collection(collection, book)`: the Book goes to the end of the Collection. A Book that is **not in the Library yet goes onto Want to read first, in the same call** (a Catalogue Book by its id; a search result as its snapshot, its Cover resolved first by the Add sheet's chain, `resolveBookCover`, then through `add_to_library`, which puts it into the Catalogue). The book page, the Library and search show the entry at once; the note about Want to read goes away.
- Tap a Collection with the check → `remove_from_collection(collection, entry)`: off the Collection, still in the Library and on her other Collections.
- New collection → a field in place of the row (placeholder `collections.namePlaceholder`) and **Create** (or Enter): `create_collection(name)`, then the Book is put on it as above. Refused names are named under the rows.
- Done, Cancel, the scrim, Escape, a swipe down → closes. Every tap was already saved.
Edge cases: the chips come from `memberships(entry)` and the member's list; a Book not in the Library has none. Adding a Book that is already on the Collection is `already_in_collection`; another member's Manual book is `book_invalid`. If a concurrent add put the same Book into the Library in between, the call uses that entry.

### Error codes of Collections
`app/data/collections.ts`, `CollectionErrorCode`; copy under `collections.error.<code>`. The database raises them as the messages of the collection functions: `name_invalid` (blank or over 80 characters), `name_taken` (she has one by that name, in any case), `collection_missing` (not one of hers, or deleted), `already_in_collection`, `not_in_collection`, `order_mismatch` (the order does not list exactly the Collection's entries), `book_invalid` (no such Book for her, or a snapshot the Catalogue refuses), `not_signed_in`; `unknown` for the rest.
`offline` (#15): no connection, nothing was sent (as the Library's).

### Offline and install  (web: `nuxt.config.ts` (`pwa`), `public/` icons from `scripts/render-icons.mjs`, `composables/useOnline.ts`, `data/deviceLibrary.ts`, `data/localData.ts`, `WriteOptions` in `data/library.ts`, `searchLibrary` in `data/search.ts`; the copy kept and restored by `stores/library.ts` and `stores/collections.ts`, the offline start in `stores/session.ts` and `plugins/session.client.ts`)
Purpose: Libellus installs to the home screen, opens without a connection on the last-loaded Library, and never pretends to save something while offline.
States: online · offline (the browser says there is no network: `navigator.onLine` false, or the backend answers none of the writes: `useOnline`, #105) · offline with no copy on the device (a first start without a connection: the Library's load error, `library.loadError` / `home.loadError`, with Retry)
Install:
- Web app manifest (`manifest.webmanifest`): name and short name `Libellus`, `display: standalone` (full-screen from the home screen, no browser chrome), portrait, `start_url`/`scope`/`id` `/`, splash and theme colour the light `surface` (manifests have no media queries; the `theme-color` tags per theme are the Theme entry's). Icons: `icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png` (`any`), `icon-maskable-512.png` (`maskable`, the mark inside the safe zone), `apple-touch-icon.png` (180, opaque) and `favicon.ico` (32 + 16). The mark is "li" in Newsreader italic in the dark ink, in the night room, the dot of the i the lamp (docs/DESIGN.md, Themes).
- App shell precached by the service worker (`@vite-pwa/nuxt`, generateSW, `registerType: autoUpdate`): the HTML of every prerendered route, all JS and CSS, the latin-subset `woff2` fonts (never the `woff` fallbacks), the manifest, the 192 icon, the apple-touch-icon and the favicon. At this commit **74 entries, 958 KiB** uncompressed (the largest a 382 KiB chunk, 114 KiB gzipped; fonts 118 KiB). Any address opens the app (`navigateFallback: '/'`).
- Covers are cached as they are seen (runtime caching, cache name `libellus-covers`): an `<img>` request (`no-cors`) to `*.mzstatic.com` or `covers.openlibrary.org`, **cache first** (a cover at an address never changes), opaque answers (status 0) and 200s kept, **bounded** to 400 covers and 180 days, oldest first, and dropped before the browser runs out of room (`purgeOnQuotaError`). A cover read with CORS (its thumbhash while a Book is added) never gets an opaque copy it could not read: it goes to the network.
The device's copy of the Library:
- After every load and every change made here (an add, a start, a finish, an abandon, a read edited or deleted, a Book removed) the member's Library is written to the device (`data/deviceLibrary.ts`, local storage): `libellus.library` holds the member (id and address), the three Status lists with each entry's Book and latest session, and the year's tally; `libellus.collections` the Collections list (sizes and first covers), every Collection opened on this device with its entries in her order, and the memberships the book pages learned. Versioned: a copy of another shape, or a torn one, is ignored and removed. A copy the storage refuses (full) is dropped, never half-written, and never an error the member sees.
- On start the stores read it back before the first frame (synchronously, which is why it is local storage and not IndexedDB): Home, Library, Collections, a Collection and the book pages of her Books show at once, no flash of an empty Library and no network needed; online a load refreshes it quietly behind them. It is only ever the member's own: a copy for another member id is not used.
- **Descriptions** (`libellus.descriptions`, `DEVICE_DESCRIPTIONS_KEY`, `saveDescriptions` / `readDescriptions` in `data/deviceLibrary.ts`; the store's `descriptions`, `descriptionOf`, `rememberDescription`): the lists leave a Book's description out, so the device keeps the descriptions of the Books in her Library apart, by Book id (`''`: none), read the first time a page needs one (not at start), and the book page reads them from there when its Book has none of its own (offline too). They come from the book page's own read (`books` by id), from what a copy written before this carried, from a snapshot's description when a Book is added, and, once the Library is loaded and the browser idle (online), from one background request per 40 Books for the Catalogue Books not held yet (`descriptions` in `data/library.ts`; the first time on a device all of them, afterwards only the ones added since). Only the Books she has are kept; signing out removes the key with the rest.
- Cleared on sign-out with everything under `libellus.` (Tab shell); also removed when the member stops being signed in without signing out here (the session ended on the server). The theme preference stays.
- Offline start: Supabase cannot renew an expired access token without a connection and retries for about half a minute before saying nobody is signed in. Offline, the member the copy belongs to opens her Library at once instead; Supabase's answer follows in the background and only a device that holds no session at all signs out. While offline a "nobody" from Supabase is not taken as a sign-out; a session that really ended signs out once the connection is back and the renewal is refused.
Offline behaviour (`useOnline`; the browser's `online`/`offline` events, so it changes without a reload):
- Nothing is asked of the network for the Library, the descriptions, the tally, the Collections list, an opened Collection or memberships while offline; when the connection comes back the lists and the Collections list refresh by themselves.
- *(Until #93; see Save offline and sync later below for what waits to sync instead. What still needs the connection keeps this label.)* **Every action that writes is disabled and labelled "Offline"** (`common.offline`, with the offline icon): the book page's Add to Library, Start reading, Finish, Read again / Start again (`book.add|start|finish|readAgain|startAgain`; DNF beside Finish is disabled and keeps its label), the Collections chip "Add to collection" (`book.addToCollection`), Finish on the Home and Library cards (`home.finish`, `library.finish`), the + on a search result (`search.add`, disabled, labelled `common.offline`), the submit of the Add, Start, Finish, DNF and manual-book sheets (`add|start|finish|abandon|manual.submit`), "Add manually" (`search.addManually`), the picker's rows, New and Create (`picker.collection|new|create`), the name sheet's action (`collectionName.action`), New collection in the top bar and the list (`collections.newTop`, `collections.new`), the Collection's Rename and Delete (`collectionOptions.rename|delete|confirmDelete`, the rows say `common.offline` as their value) its reordering (`collection.grip` disabled; drag, hold and arrow keys do nothing), and the reading history's writes (#11): the Edit sheet's Save and Delete this read (`editSession.submit|delete`), Remove from Library in the book's options (`bookOptions.remove`, the row says `common.offline`), and the action of both confirmations (`deleteSession.confirm`, `removeEntry.confirm`; Cancel still closes them). A sheet that is open when the connection goes stays open with its action saying Offline. Reading, navigating, the theme switch and signing out stay available.
- Search answers from her own Library (the entries this device last saw: Currently reading, Want to read, Finished), at once, by title and author (every query word begins a word of the title or an author; `matchQuality` above "some words") or by ISBN, best match first; each row shows its Status. One quiet line says so where the hint would be: `search.offlineNote`. Nothing of hers matches → `search.noResultsTitle`, `search.offlineNoResults` with the query, and the note; no way to add one by hand. No source is asked.
- Writes go through the repositories, and the repositories refuse them offline (`createLibrary` / `createCollections` / `createManualBooks` take `{ online }`; a write while offline returns `offline` before anything is sent): since #93 a write that can wait goes into the outbox there instead (`{ queue }`, Save offline and sync later), without changing a page or a store.
Copy keys: `common.offline`, `search.offlineNote`, `search.offlineNoResults`, `library.error.offline`, `collections.error.offline`
IDs: the action IDs above (disabled while offline), `search.offline`
Flow: `web/e2e/offline.spec.ts` (a member with a Book on each Status loads her Library online → the device goes offline (`context.setOffline(true)`) → reload → the Library is still there in all three segments, Finish on the Library and Home cards disabled and labelled Offline → search finds her own Book by title and by author with the note, a Book she does not have is not found → her book page opens with Start reading disabled → Remove from Library is disabled too → back online the action is back without a reload → removing the Book takes it out of the device's copy at once → sign out leaves no `libellus.` key). Chromium, not WebKit: WebKit's driver cannot answer a page load from a route while offline. The dev server has no service worker, so the flow keeps the files the page loaded and answers them offline; the real service worker was checked against `nuxt generate` + a static server in Chromium (73 precached entries before #11 landed, both covers in `libellus-covers`, an offline reload opening the Library, a book page reloaded offline). Persistence, restore, the member check, clearing on sign-out, a torn or old copy, a full storage, writes refused offline (the history's edit, delete and remove included) and the offline search in `web/tests/offline.test.ts`.
Native: the copy is the platform's own store (a file or a small database) read before the first screen; connectivity from the platform's path monitor; the same `offline` refusal in the repositories; install is the app itself.

### Save offline and sync later  (web: `data/queuedWrites.ts`, `data/outbox.ts`, `clearLocalDatabase` in `data/localData.ts`, `WriteOptions.queue` in `data/library.ts` and `data/collections.ts`, `stores/sync.ts`, `rebase`/`applyWrites` in `stores/library.ts`, `readsOf` in `stores/history.ts`, `components/shell/{SyncChip,SyncSheet}.vue` in `components/shell/Header.vue`, `public/sw-sync.js` (`pwa.workbox.importScripts`); database: `sync_write`, `synced_writes`)
Purpose: issue #93. A change made without a connection is no longer refused: it is saved on the device, shown at once, and sent to the database in order once the connection is back. Nothing that waits is shown as synced, and what the database refuses is undone and said.
Which writes wait (queueable: they name rows the member already has, so the database function needs nothing the device does not hold) and which stay online-only:

| Write (RPC) | Offline | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `update_progress` (Update progress, her own total, Undo, incl. `p_clear`) | waits | entry id; the day is booked on the window's first day if it waited more than a day (`sync_write`) |
| `start_reading`, `read_again` | wait | entry id; the new read gets a local id until it syncs |
| `finish_reading`, `abandon_reading` | wait | entry id |
| `update_session` (Edit a read) | waits | session id (a local one is rewritten once its start syncs) |
| `remove_from_library` | waits | entry id |
| `set_entry_hidden` (Hide from followers, the Book's ⋯) | waits | entry id; the one follow-related write that waits |
| `add_to_library` from a Catalogue Book (has an id, not a Manual book) | waits | the snapshot is the Catalogue's; the entry gets a local id until it syncs |
| `add_to_collection` (a Book already in the Library), `remove_from_collection`, `reorder_collection`, `rename_collection`, `delete_collection` | wait | Collection and entry ids the device holds |
| `save_reader_highlight` (the reader's highlights, #131) | waits | one row by a device-made id; last write wins, so a late one never overwrites a newer one; no Library entry is shown or read again |
| `add_to_library` from a search result | waits, but only as the palette's optimistic add | the snapshot has no Catalogue id yet, so the device shows it under its page key (`apple-…`, `ol-…`, `isbn-…`) and the outbox resolves its Cover just before sending (`coverPending`, `prepare`). From the book page it stays online-only, and its button says Offline |
| `add_to_collection` of a Book not in the Library | online-only | it is an add from a search result as well |
| `create_collection` | online-only | a new row other writes would have to name; kept simple |
| `delete_session` | online-only | the Status follows from the reads that are left, which the device may not all hold |
| `change_edition` | online-only | the editions are looked up online, a new Book's Cover resolved |
| `add_manual_book`, `import_books`, search, Goodreads | online-only | they need sources on the network |
| `mute_series`, `unmute_series` | online-only | a mute is a rule of the series, like her series corrections (`set_entry_series`): not queued, the controls say Offline |
| Name (`auth.updateUser`), theme | online-only / local | the name is the account's; the theme never leaves the device |

How (data layer):
- The repositories take `{ online, queue }`. While the queue holds (offline, the outbox not read yet, or earlier writes still waiting, so nothing overtakes them) a queueable write is not sent: its arguments, exactly those of the online RPC, go into the outbox with an id made on the device, and the repository answers with the entry as the database will have it (`applyWrite`). What the database would refuse on the device's copy (`not_reading`, `already_reading`, `progress_invalid` …) is refused at once and does not wait.
- The outbox is per member, in IndexedDB (`libellus` database, `outbox` store; `LOCAL_DATABASE`), written whole on every change. Sent oldest first through `sync_write(p_request_id, p_action, p_args)`, which calls the same function as the online path and records the id in the same transaction: a write sent twice (an answer lost) is applied once, answered from the record (`replayed`). Rows a write made come back as `entry_id`/`session_id`; the outbox keeps the device's id → the database's and rewrites every write behind it.
- A send that gets no answer (status 0) or a server error (5xx, 401, 408, 429) stops the line there and is tried again after 2 s, 4 s, 8 s … up to 5 min (`backoff`); a server error 8 times in a row becomes a failure (`unknown`). A refusal (any code the database raised by name) drops that write as a failure (`SyncFailure`: what, about which Book or Collection, the code); the writes behind it are still sent.
- The idempotency record is swept daily: `purge_synced_writes()` deletes the `synced_writes` rows older than 30 days (a write never waits that long), run by a pg_cron job (`purge-synced-writes`, 03:30 UTC) where pg_cron exists; `sync_write` still drops one member's rows older than 60 days as she syncs.
- A connection that answers nothing (#105; `data/network.ts`, `composables/useOnline.ts`): the browser's `online` flag only knows there is no network at all. A write to the REST API (a POST, PATCH or DELETE; not a read, not the auth calls, not `search_books`, `import_books` or `invite_code_status`) is given 8 s to be answered (`WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `withWriteTimeout` in the client's `fetch`) and is cut off after that: supabase-js reports it as status 0 like any network failure (`isNoAnswer`). Such a write is **not an error to show**: a write that can wait goes into the outbox as if the device had known it was offline (`unanswered` in `queued`, in the Library and Collections repositories) and shows the entry as it will be; one that cannot wait (Change edition, Delete a read, a manual book, a new Collection) is refused as `offline`. Either way `useOnline` turns false (`reportNoAnswer`) and every write says "Offline" until the backend answers again: a reachability probe (`createProbe`: `GET /auth/v1/health` with the anon key, 4 s, any answer below 500) is asked after 2 s, 4 s, 8 s … up to 30 s (the pause grows with each write that got no answer and starts over when a write is answered), and the browser's `online` event asks at once. A write that is answered, a refusal included, ends it (`reportAnswer`). The probe also runs before every flush (`reachable` in `createOutbox`): when nothing answers nothing is sent, so no waiting write is held up for a whole timeout, the device counts as offline, and the flush is tried again after the same doubling pause. A write whose answer was lost after the server took it and that then waits is sent again through `sync_write` under a new id; the database refuses what no longer fits (`not_reading` …) and it shows as a failure like any refusal.
- **An add from the search palette is optimistic** (`AddHow.optimistic` in `data/library.ts`, `openAdd(book, { optimistic })` / `confirmAdd` in `stores/library.ts`): it goes into the outbox **even online with nothing waiting** (`WriteQueue.open`: somebody is signed in), is answered with the entry as it will be, and is sent at once by the same flush (`queue.add` flushes when online). Ordering is the outbox's: while the add waits the line holds, so a write made on the new entry (start, finish, progress, a Collection) queues behind it and finds the database's id (`creates`, local → real rewrite); nothing overtakes it. The entry is under the device's id and its Book under the page key until the database answers: `applyWrites` matches an add to an entry by the Catalogue id or by one of the Book's source keys (`holdsBook`), so a read that lands after the database took it and before the line let go does not show it twice. After a flush the Library read lays the waiting writes over the database's rows and `reconcile` tells the pages and search what changed: an entry that is gone (refused) leaves its pages' keys and its result's mark, one that took its place (the database's) is what they show from then on. The ebooks store leaves such entries out of matching until they have synced (a link kept on the device must name the database's ids) and matches the waiting files once the line is empty. The offline copy of the entry is the same (`libellus.library`), so a reload shows it.
- Sent when: the app starts with a connection (once the outbox is read), the `online` event, the app coming back to the foreground (`visibilitychange`), right after a write while online, the retry timer, and the service worker's Background Sync (`libellus-outbox`; where the browser has it, the worker tells the open window to sync; the writes need the member's session, so with no window open they go on the next start).
- After a flush that synced or refused something the Library is read again and the writes still waiting are laid over it (`applyWrites`): a refused write's change disappears (the rollback), a synced one stays; the tally, open histories, the reading days and the Collections are read again. While writes wait, the Collections, memberships and an open history are not refreshed from the database (the device's copy stands). The device's copy of the Library (`libellus.library`) holds the waiting changes too, so a reload offline shows them; it is laid over again once the outbox is read, for a copy saved before a write that waited.
- Signing out deletes the device's IndexedDB database with the waiting writes (a shared phone). So with writes waiting, Sign out (Profile, `profile.signOut`) asks first (`signOutUnsynced`, `UiConfirm` with an `alternative`): "N changes have not synced yet" / "If you sign out now, they are lost." with **Sync first** (online only, `signOutUnsynced.alternative`: sends everything now, pauses or not, and signs out once nothing waits; if something cannot go, `signOutUnsynced.error` says so and she can try again), **Sign out anyway** (`signOutUnsynced.confirm`) and Cancel. Offline only Sign out anyway and Cancel.
States: nothing waiting (no chip) · waiting (chip "Waiting to sync (N)") · syncing (the sheet says "Syncing N changes…") · refused (chip "Couldn't sync (N)" in the error colour once nothing waits; the sheet lists them).
Screens: since #93 these stay available offline (no "Offline" label): Update progress on Home, the Library card and the book page (`home.update`, `home.undo`, `book.updateProgress`, `book.progressTotal`, the sheet's `progress.action`/`progress.finish`), Finish (`home.finish`, `library.finish`, `progress.finish`, `finish.submit`), DNF (`progress.abandon`, `abandon.submit`), Start / Read again / Start again (`book.start`, `book.readAgain`, `book.startAgain`, `start.submit`), Save in the Edit read sheet (`editSession.submit`), Remove from Library (`bookOptions.remove`, `removeEntry.confirm`), Hide from followers (`bookOptions.hide`), Add for a Catalogue Book (`book.add`, `add.submit`), the Collections chip and the picker's rows for a Book in the Library (`book.addToCollection`, `picker.collection`), Rename and Delete a Collection (`collectionOptions.rename|delete|confirmDelete`, `collectionName.action` when renaming) and reordering (`collection.grip`). Still "Offline": `search.add`, `search.addManually`, `manual.submit`, `book.add`/`add.submit` for a search result or Manual book, `picker.new|create`, `collections.new|newTop`, `collectionName.action` when creating, `bookOptions.changeEdition`, `edition.action`, `editSession.delete`, `deleteSession.confirm`, `import.*`, `accountName.action`. Every follow write is online-only and says "Offline" (`common.offline`): `follow` (Follow, Ask to follow, withdrawing a request), Accept, Decline, Follow back, Unfollow, Remove as follower, Block, Unblock, New link (`followLink.renew`), each privacy switch (`set_private`, `set_social_sections`), and the Circle's Accept and Decline.
- The sync chip (`shell.sync`, its text `shell.syncLabel`) sits at the leading end of the header's controls row, opposite the avatar, on the tab screens; hidden when nothing waits and nothing failed. A tap opens the sync sheet (`sync`): `sync.waiting` (how many, and that they go by themselves), the waiting writes oldest first (`sync.item`: what, `sync.itemAbout` the Book or Collection in the serif), then the failures (`sync.failure`: `sync.failureAbout`, `sync.failureText` "Couldn't sync: Finished", `sync.failureReason` the action's own error copy, `sync.dismiss` ✕), or `sync.allSynced`. Undo of a progress save (5 s) works offline: it is one more write in the line.
Copy keys: `profile.signOutUnsynced.title|text|syncFirst|syncing|signOut|failed`, `sync.chip`, `sync.chipFailed`, `sync.chipLabel`, `sync.title`, `sync.waiting`, `sync.syncing`, `sync.pendingTitle`, `sync.failedTitle`, `sync.failedNote`, `sync.failedItem`, `sync.dismissLabel`, `sync.allSynced`, `sync.action.<rpc>`, and `library.error.*` / `collections.error.*` for the reasons
IDs: `signOutUnsynced` (`.title`, `.text`, `.alternative`, `.confirm`, `.cancel`, `.error`), `shell.sync`, `shell.syncLabel`, `sync`, `sync.cancel`, `sync.waiting`, `sync.item`, `sync.itemAbout`, `sync.failure`, `sync.failureAbout`, `sync.failureText`, `sync.failureReason`, `sync.dismiss`, `sync.allSynced`
Flows: `web/tests/optimistic-add.test.ts` and `web/tests/no-answer.test.ts` (timeout and watcher, probe, the repositories queueing an unanswered write, the outbox probing before it sends), `web/e2e/offline-sync.spec.ts` (progress updated offline → chip "Waiting to sync (1)" → reload offline: value and chip still there, the sheet lists it → online: synced by itself, the database has it, the chip goes; Sign out with a change waiting offline: asked, Cancel keeps it, Sign out anyway drops it and the outbox; online with the sync call failing: Sync first says it could not, then syncs and signs out), `web/e2e/offline.spec.ts` (what stays available and what says Offline). Data layer, including a change refused on sync and Save and Undo offline: `web/tests/optimistic-add.test.ts` (the optimistic add against the stack: answered at once, nothing in the database until the send, Status and first read, a double tap, order behind it, the Cover prepared once before the send and a Book still sent when it cannot be, a refusal as a failure with nothing left to show, no duplicate while a read lands mid-sync, offline, not optimistic stays online-only, no line, a Manual book), `web/tests/outbox.test.ts` (order, writes queued behind waiting ones while online, local ids rewritten, refusal as a failure with the rest still sent, no double apply after a lost answer, backoff and wake, give-up, restart, one send at a time, Collections, `applyWrite`/`applyWrites`). Database: `supabase/tests/sync_write_test.sql`. Checked on the Android emulator (Chrome, airplane mode) — screenshots in the PR.
Native: the same outbox in the platform's own store (a file or SQLite), the same `sync_write` call and request ids, connectivity from the path monitor, and the platform's background task (BGTaskScheduler, WorkManager) instead of Background Sync.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android

### Fable import  (no screen; web: `scripts/import-fable.ts`, `scripts/fable/`, `app/data/import/`)
Purpose: the owner's one-off move from Fable (#17), and the dev seed (`pnpm seed:dev`). An owner's script run as the service role, not a member action: nothing for a native port to copy.
What a member sees afterwards: ordinary Library entries, Books and Reading sessions. Editions of one title by one author are one entry (the most recent edition is the Book); every Fable read is one session, after the owner's overrides: the tracker's `read` shelf finished, `dnf` abandoned (ending on its DNF date, else its latest known day), and on any other shelf a start without a finish or DNF date open, so *Currently reading* (the tracker has no such shelf); *Want to read* has none; ratings are the exact quarters; the Status is derived by the database as for any entry. Imported Books carry their real source (`apple` when Apple has the edition by ISBN, `openlibrary` when only OpenLibrary has it), so another member who finds one in search adds the same Catalogue Book; a few editions neither knows stay `import`, which `add_to_library` accepts as the Catalogue row it is (an `import` snapshot matching no row is refused, so only the import makes them), and a book Fable knew without any ISBN or id becomes the member's Manual book.
Covers: high-resolution, from Apple (exact edition, else by title in the storefront of the language read) or OpenLibrary, with thumbhash and colours as `add_to_library` stores them; otherwise the Placeholder cover. Never Fable's (Fable is going away), never the German National Library's (browsers get a bot page; found and reported for rehosting later).
Idempotent: `library_entries.import_key` and `reading_sessions.import_key` (`fable:<tracker id>`, written only by the import) find everything again; a rerun updates what changed, deletes imported sessions the plan no longer has, adopts an entry the member made in the app for the same Book, and never touches sessions made in the app.
Tests: the mapping in `web/tests/fable-mapping.test.ts` (synthetic fixture JSON), covers in `web/tests/fable-covers.test.ts` (made-up answers, no live APIs), the writes and reruns in `web/tests/fable-import.test.ts` (local stack), the keys in `supabase/tests/import_keys_test.sql`
- [x] Web (script)
### Regal export  (no screen; web: `scripts/export-regal.ts`, `app/data/export/`)
Purpose: the owner's Library as a Regal library file (version 2) for the 3D bookshelf on fabkho.dev/books (#22). An owner's script, read only, run as the service role (`--target local|hosted`, hosted only with `--confirm-host`); nothing for a native port to copy except the pure mapping if a client ever exports.
Mapping (`app/data/export/regal.ts`): one Book per Library entry, `id` = the Book's id; status from the latest session (none `to-read`, open `currently-reading`, finished `read`, abandoned `dnf`); `dateStarted` from the latest session, `dateRead`/`rating` (quarters ÷ 4)/`review` from the last finished read (a never-finished Book: its latest read's review), `readCount` = finished reads, `dateAdded` = the entry's day in the given zone; ISBNs, pages, year, publisher, blurb as stored; `assets.front` = the Cover URL (http(s) only), `assets.palette` from the Cover's two colours by Regal's rules. Left out: series, binding, original year, genre, quotes, spoiler flag, thumbhash; empty values are omitted, never null. Books sorted by id; the same Library gives the same file. The script exports Finished and Currently reading by default (`--statuses` for Want to read) and, with `--carry-art <published file>`, keeps the art Regal shows now for every Book it has (`app/data/export/carryArt.ts`: by ISBN-13, else bare title + first author's surname).
Validation: Regal's validator, vendored in `app/data/export/regalLibraryFile.ts` (Regal commit e1842aa); the script writes nothing invalid.
Tests: `web/tests/regal-export.test.ts` (mapping against `tests/fixtures/regal/libellus-export.json`, carry-art, palette, the read against the local stack), `web/tests/regal-library-file.test.ts` (the port against Regal's fixtures; against Regal's own validator with `REGAL_DIR`)
- [x] Web (script)

### Import books  (web: `app/pages/import.vue`, `components/import/{Counts,Progress,NoteList,Shelves,HowTo,EditionSheet}.vue`, `stores/import.ts`, `data/bookImport.ts`, `data/import/{csv,rows,detect,editions,goodreads,hardcover}.ts`; database: `import_books`, `import_place_in_collections`; the row in `components/profile/Account.vue`)
Purpose: a member brings her reading history from another app's export (#40, #111): Goodreads' library export or Hardcover's CSV export (also a file written in Hardcover's custom import format, the same columns). She never picks the app: the file's header tells (`data/import/detect.ts`), so there is no format picker. Fable is covered through Goodreads: the browser extensions Fable members use (ShelfBridge, Fable Xport) write Goodreads-compatible CSV. No source logos. One normalised row model (`data/import/rows.ts`: title, authors, ISBN-13/10, edition hints — format, pages, year, language, publisher —, Status, the row's read, earlier reads with or without days, rating in quarters, review, Collections, added day) with an adapter per app (`goodreads.ts`, `hardcover.ts`); everything after the adapter (preview, lookups, `import_books`) knows no app. A new app is one more adapter.
Layout: a pushed screen in the tab layout, reached from the Profile's account rows (**Import books**, between the Dark mode switch and Sign out); its back falls back to the Profile. The round back button, the title `import.title` large, in the sans like Collections' title (serif is for book titles). Then by state: **pick** — the lamp over the empty shelf (`UiEmptyState`): `import.pickTitle`, the one line naming the apps it reads and what comes along (`import.pickText`), the primary **Choose CSV file**, a file error under it, then **How to export** (`ImportHowTo`: an eyebrow over a row group, one row per app — `import.app.goodreads|hardcover` — with a chevron; a tap opens its one line of steps in place, `import.howTo.<app>`, and turns the chevron down; one open at a time). **Looking up / preview / importing** — the file name as a mono eyebrow, `import.inFile` in the serif (the app the header told and the count: "From Hardcover · 312 books"), the books per Status as three light figures between hairlines (`ImportCounts`, as Home's tally writes a count), then while looking up the running count `import.matching` over a hairline track lit in the accent (`ImportProgress`) with **Choose another file**; in the preview a row group with `import.matched`, `import.fromFile`, `import.alreadyThere` (mono counts), the primary **Import N books** (`import.start`) or `import.nothingToImport`, **Choose another file**, then **Your shelves** (`ImportShelves`, when the file has shelves other than read / to-read / currently-reading / a did-not-finish one; for Hardcover **Your lists**, `import.listsTitle|listsText`, her lists and Owned): `import.shelvesTitle`, `import.shelvesText`, one row per shelf with its book count (`import.shelfCount`) and D's check (`role=checkbox`, as the Collection picker), most books first, all ticked; while importing the same group and `import.importing` with its track. Under all of them **Needs a look** (eyebrow with the count): one row per book not found, found only by its title, or carried over with a change, and per skipped row — the cover of the edition it will be added as (the Placeholder when there is none), the serif title, the author, and the notes in words (`import.note.*`; `byTitleEdition` names the year and language of the edition a title search picked, so she knows it is not an exact one). A book found only by its title, or only in the file, carries **Choose edition** (`import.chooseEdition`) under its notes once every book is looked up; it opens the Change edition sheet (see Choose edition below). A book whose edition she chose moves out of Needs a look to **Your choices** (eyebrow with the count, `import.choices`, under Needs a look): its cover, title, author, a mono line of the chosen edition's language · year · pages · publisher (`import.choicesList.facts`), the note `import.note.chosen` (or `chosenInLibrary`) and **Change edition** (`import.changeEdition`). **Done** — the first covers of what was added as a small overlapping pile, `import.doneTitle`, `import.doneText` (or `import.doneNothing`), a row group `import.added`, `import.kept` (and `import.failed`, `import.notReached` when there are any), a stop message if a call failed, the primary **Go to Library** and **Import another file**, then the rows not imported with their reason (`library.error.<code>`).
States: pick · reading the file (`import.reading`, the button locked) · file error (`import.fileError.notSupported|notCsv|storygraph|librarything|bookshelf|missingColumns|empty|unreadable|unknown`: a spreadsheet or other non-text file is `notCsv`; a CSV of no supported app is `notSupported`, or names the app when its header tells (StoryGraph, LibraryThing, Bookshelf: not read yet); a Goodreads or Hardcover export without a column its rows need is `missingColumns`, naming the app and the columns) · looking up (count running, the preview filling in) · preview · nothing to import · importing (count running) · done · done with failures or stopped · offline (Choose and Import read `common.offline`, disabled; Choose edition still opens, see below).
Actions → result:
- Choose CSV file → the platform's file picker (`.csv`). The file is read on the device (`parseExport`, PapaParse: quoted fields with commas, quotes and line breaks, a BOM, Excel's `sep=;` line, CRLF; header cells in any order and case). **Which app**: each adapter weighs the columns that point to it (3 for one only that app writes — Goodreads `Exclusive Shelf`, `Author l-f`, `Bookshelves with positions`; Hardcover `Hardcover Book ID`, `Hardcover Edition ID`, `Review Slate` — less for shared ones); the best score from 3 up decides; then its required columns (Goodreads `Title`, `Author`, `Exclusive Shelf`; Hardcover `Title`, `Author`, `Status`) must be there, else `missingColumns`. **Goodreads** (`="…"` ISBNs, `Average Rating` present or not): Rows map as: `read` → Finished with one finished read ending on Date Read (or undated), no start, My Rating × 4 quarters (0 = unrated), My Review as plain text; `currently-reading` → Currently reading, open since Date Added (today without one); `to-read` and any other shelf → Want to read, a custom shelf noted. Titles lose their series (`Howling Dark (Sun Eater, #2)` → `Howling Dark`). A day that is no day or after today is left out and noted; a rating or review on a book that is not finished is left out and noted; a review over 10,000 characters is shortened; a row without a title or a second row with the same Book Id is skipped. Each row's key is `goodreads:<Book Id>` (exports without Book Ids: the ISBN, else title and author). **Hardcover** (`Title, Author, Series, Status, Privacy, Hardcover Book ID, Hardcover Edition ID, ISBN 10, ISBN 13, ASIN, Media, Country Code, Language Code, Binding, Pages, Duration in Seconds, Publish Date, Publisher, Genres, Moods, Tags, Content Warnings, Lists, Date Added, Date Started, Date Finished, Rating, Review, Review Contains Spoilers, Sponsored Review, Review Date, Review URL, Review Media URL, Private Notes, Owned, Compilation, Review Slate`): Status in any case — `Read` → Finished, the latest read (n-th Date Started with the n-th Date Finished, comma-separated; a finish alone is a read without a start) is the row's, with Rating (½ stars → quarters) and Review (`\n` a line break), the reads before it earlier finished reads with their days (`import.note.earlierReads`, at most 20); `Did Not Finish` → the latest read abandoned, without its rating; `Currently Reading` → open from its latest start without a finish (Date Added, else today), finished reads before it as earlier reads; `Paused` the same, noted (`import.note.paused`; Libellus has no pause); `Want to Read` and anything else → Want to read; `None` / `Ignored` → skipped (`import.note.notShelved`). Authors without the credits in brackets that are not the author's (`(Narrator)`, `(Translator)`); Series and Lists without their positions (`(#2.0)`); Lists → Collections offered, `Owned` (`true` or `Yes`) → the Collection "Owned". Edition hints: ISBN 13/10, Language Code (ranks a title search's editions before the title's little words do), Media or Binding (`Ebook` ranks Apple's ebooks first), Pages, the year of Publish Date, Publisher. Genres, moods, tags, content warnings, private notes, privacy, spoiler and sponsored flags, ASIN stay out. Key `hardcover:<Hardcover Book ID>` (without one, the ISBN, else title and author).
- The file (#111): read as UTF-8, else Windows-1252 (a file a spreadsheet saved again), its delimiter guessed (`,` `;` tab). Per row: `read` → one finished read on Date Read; a did-not-finish exclusive shelf (`dnf`, `did-not-finish`, `abandoned`, `gave-up`, `abgebrochen` …) → one abandoned read on Date Read, its review kept, its rating left out (`import.note.abandonedRating`); `currently-reading` → an open read from Date Added; anything else Want to read. Read Count − 1 earlier reads, finished and undated (`import.note.extraReads`, at most 20). Ratings to the nearest quarter star (halves from other apps); days `YYYY/MM/DD`, `YYYY-MM-DD` or `DD.MM.YYYY`, no time zone applied. An ISBN a spreadsheet turned into a number (`9.78E+12`) is no ISBN (`import.note.isbnMangled`). Shelves other than the Status ones, a custom exclusive one included, are offered as Collections. Rows of the same work (title without series and subtitle, first author's surname) under different keys know each other's keys: two editions she shelved (an English and a German read), never taken for each other. Private notes stay out.
- Then what she has: the keys of her imported entries with their entry ids (rows imported before are not looked up again) and her Library entries. Then every other row's edition, **four rows at a time**, each source call with a 15 s deadline, Apple's requests spaced 250 ms and a refused one (403/429/503) tried twice more: the Catalogue by ISBN → Apple Books then OpenLibrary by ISBN (the exact edition) → a title + first author search over all three, taking the result of the same work (same title without brackets and subtitle, same author surname; a title in another script is a translation) that fits best, by what the file itself says (no one else is asked about the row): the same language (told from the titles' little words), an Apple ebook for a Kindle Binding, about the same page count, the same year; ties keep the search's order → the file's own fields: an `import` Book keyed by its ISBN-13 (also when a title search found the work: the exact edition she shelved wins, and lends the found edition's cover when its own ISBN finds none), or without an ISBN her own Manual book. A row nothing was found for is tried once more at the end. A row not found by its ISBN keeps the same work's editions its title search found (up to 20, `Edition.alternatives`), best fit first: the order the match picked in, so the first is the one it took. Verdicts: matched (an edition found by ISBN or by title), from the file, already in her Library (imported before, or the same Book / ISBN / source id; for a Manual book the same title and author).
- Import N books → the Cover of each Book new to the Catalogue is resolved as on add (`resolveBookCover`: Apple artwork → OpenLibrary by cover or ISBN → Placeholder), then **one database call per ten rows**, `import_books(p_rows)`: each row in its own savepoint — the Book found by id (a Catalogue Book or her own Manual book), or added from its snapshot (`apple`/`openlibrary`/`import`, found again by ISBN-13 or source id; `manual` private to her), the entry with the row's key, `added_at` the Date Added (midday UTC; now when later) and the file's page count as her own (`page_count_override`) when the Book says another or none, its read with the same key (`outcome: abandoned` for a did-not-finish shelf), `earlier_reads` (Hardcover: `[{started_on, ended_on}]`, oldest first) and then `extra_reads` (Goodreads' undated ones) as earlier finished reads keyed `<key>#2` … made to sort before it (together at most 20, none on Want to read: `session_invalid`; one ending before it starts: `ended_before_started`), and the entry put on the Collections of the shelves she kept (`collections`: found by name in any case, or made at the end of her list; also for a row imported before or already hers); the Status derived by the database. Per row: `added`, `imported` (that key is in her Library: the same file twice adds nothing), `in_library` (she has the Book: her entry stays, no second read) or `failed` with the code. The count runs per call; the Library reloads.
- Choose another file / Import another file → back to pick (a running lookup is abandoned). Go to Library → `/library`. Back → where she came from (Home without history).
Edge cases: a call that fails stops the import with `import.stopped`; what was written stays and importing again adds only the rest. Offline before choosing or importing: disabled with `common.offline`; going offline mid-import stops it between calls with `offline`. A slow network makes the count slower, never stuck: a source that does not answer in time is left out for that row. Leaving the page keeps it running (the store holds it). Signing out (or another member signing in) forgets the file. Two rows the lookups resolve to the same Book: the second is `in_library`.
A book she has under another edition (#104): besides the key and the Book the lookup finds, a row whose **work title** (brackets and subtitle dropped, accents and case ignored) and **first author's surname** match a Book in her Library counts as `import.alreadyThere` and is not looked up or added again (after Change edition the entry holds another edition than the file's ISBN finds). A finished row with an end day only matches when her entry's latest read did not end on another day (an entry without a read, or a read without a day, still matches). The preview decides it from her Library (`libraryTitleIndex`), the database again when writing (`import_books`: `file_title`, `file_author` per row → `in_library`; helpers `work_title_key`, `author_surname_key`), so another client gets the same answer. An entry imported from one of the file's other rows of the work (`other_keys`) never matches (#111). **Two apps, one library** (#111): importing Hardcover's export after Goodreads' (or the other way round) adds no second entry and no read: a row's key differs, but the same Book (ISBN) or this work-title rule finds her entry first (`in_library`; a row's earlier reads only shape a new entry). The keys of imported entries are read for every supported app (`importedKeys`: `goodreads:`, `hardcover:`), page by page. Accepted: the two apps writing a different finish day for the same read (her edit in one of them) may import a second entry, as below. Accepted: a member who edited the dates or the title of that book and imports again may get a second entry. Tests: `supabase/tests/import_books_test.sql`, `supabase/tests/import_books_reads_shelves_test.sql`, `web/tests/goodreads-import.test.ts`, `web/tests/goodreads-real-exports.test.ts` (the synthetic `tests/fixtures/goodreads/battle_export.csv`, modelled on real exports). Measuring a real export on the local stack (counts only; the file stays local): `web/scripts/import-battle.ts`.
Copy keys: `import.*` (`import.edition.*` for the sheet), `status.*`, `library.error.*`, `common.offline`, `common.etAl`, `book.edition.listLabel|loading|failed|retry|ebook`
IDs: `shell.import`, `import.back`, `import.title`, `import.emptyTitle`, `import.empty`, `import.choose`, `import.howTo`, `import.howTo.goodreads|hardcover` (+ `.text`), `import.file`, `import.fileError`, `import.preview`, `import.fileName`, `import.inFile`, `import.counts`, `import.count.want_to_read|reading|finished`, `import.matching` (+ `.label`), `import.cancel`, `import.matched`, `import.fromFile`, `import.alreadyThere`, `import.start`, `import.nothing`, `import.another`, `import.importing` (+ `.label`), `import.shelves`, `import.shelf`, `import.shelfName`, `import.attention`, `import.attentionList` (+ `.row`, `.title`, `.note`, `.action`), `import.choices`, `import.choicesList` (+ `.row`, `.title`, `.facts`, `.note`, `.action`), `edition` and its `edition.*` ids (the sheet's, see Change edition), `edition.hint`, `edition.offline`, `import.done`, `import.doneCovers`, `import.doneTitle`, `import.added`, `import.kept`, `import.failedCount`, `import.notReached`, `import.stopped`, `import.toLibrary`, `import.again`, `import.failedList` (+ `.row`, `.title`, `.note`)

#### Choose edition in the import preview  (#111; web: `components/import/EditionSheet.vue` over `components/book/EditionPicker.vue`, `stores/import.ts`: `openChoice`, `pick`, `confirmChoice`; `editionChoices`, `fileEdition`, `Edition.alternatives` in `data/bookImport.ts`, `rankEditions` in `data/import/editions.ts`)
Purpose: a book the match could not be sure of is hers to settle before it is imported, with the sheet she knows from the Book page (Change edition) and no detour through a Book page (the Book is not in her Library yet). The pick changes the preview only; nothing is written until **Import N books**, which then writes that edition. Books she does not touch import what the match picked, as before.
Entry point: **Choose edition** on a row under Needs a look that was found only by its title or exists only in the file (`import.attentionList.action`), shown once every book is looked up (not while looking up or importing); **Change edition** on a row under Your choices (`import.choicesList.action`). Rows found by their ISBN (the exact edition) and skipped rows have none.
Layout: the Change edition sheet itself (`BookEditionPicker`, ids `edition.*`, a labelled radio group `book.edition.listLabel`), titled **Choose edition** (`import.edition.title`) with the action **Choose** (`import.edition.action`, `edition.action`) and the hint `import.edition.hint` ("Pick the edition you hold. Only this preview changes; the import adds the one you pick."). Rows in this order: the edition the preview has now (picked, "Imported as", `import.edition.current`); the book **as in the file** (`import.edition.file`; no source's edition: the `import` Book of its ISBN, or her own Manual book without one; it wears the cover of the work's best edition when it has none of its own); the editions the match found, best fit first (language of the file, ebook for a Kindle binding, page count, year: the order the match ranks in); then what the edition search finds — the same search as the Book page's, by title and first author, the work's OpenLibrary editions when the edition found has a work key — added at the end in the same ranking, never moving a row that is shown. A row that is the same edition or looks the same as one listed is left out. Under the list: `edition.loading` while sources answer, `edition.failed` with **Try again**, `edition.none` ("No other editions found.") when there is only the current one. Last, **My edition isn't listed** (`edition.missing`): the sheet makes way for finding the row's edition by its ISBN or making it herself (see My edition isn't listed); what she takes there becomes the row's edition in the preview, as a pick does. No Format row here (the Book page's).
Actions: tap a row → the ✓ moves to it; **Choose** is disabled until a row other than the current one is picked → the sheet closes and the row's edition is replaced in the preview: the row leaves Needs a look and appears under Your choices with the edition's facts; it counts as Matched (**Matched to an edition**), or as **Added from the file** when she kept it as in the file, or as already in her Library when the edition she picked is one she has (`import.note.chosenInLibrary`; it is then not imported). Cancel / swipe down / Escape → nothing changes. Changing a choice again opens the sheet on her current pick.
Offline: choosing writes nothing, so it works offline with the editions the match found; the search for more needs the network and the sheet says so (`edition.offline`: `import.edition.offline`) instead of "Looking for other editions…"; it runs by itself when the connection returns.
Edge cases: choosing is closed while looking up (a retry of a row would overwrite it) and when **Import** starts; a new file or another member forgets every choice; the sheet and the Book page's Change edition are one component, so a change to one's rows (facts, covers, radio group) is the other's too.
Flow: no flow; the data in `web/tests/import-edition-choice.test.ts` (alternatives ranked as the match ranks, the sheet's opening rows, the file's own edition, the write of the picked edition).
Native: a pushed view from the account menu; the file comes from the document picker; the same RPC.

### Reading history  (web: `components/book/History.vue`, `stores/history.ts`, in `app/pages/book/[key].vue`)
Purpose: every read of a Book in the Library on its page, newest first (D's book-finished timeline; issue #11; issue #1, story 25).
Layout: on the book page under the Collections row and above About: the eyebrow `history.title` with the count opposite (`history.count`, "3 reads"), then a thin thread with a node per read. The read in progress has a lit lamp node; the others a hairline ring. Per read: the name (`history.readingNow` for the open one, `history.thisRead` when there is only one read, otherwise `history.ordinal.first|second|third|fourth|fifth`, then `history.nth` "Read 6"), beside it the outcome in small faint type (`status.finished`, or a slash icon and `status.notFinished`), the Rating as small stars with its value opposite; underneath the days in mono (`3 Oct – 10 Oct`, the year shown once at the end or on both ends when they differ and outside this year; `history.since` for an open read; one date alone when a read began and ended on the same day; `history.startedOn|endedOn` when only one day is known; `history.undated` when neither) and the days (`history.days` "1 day | N days" for a closed read, `history.day` "day N" for the open one, as the status line says it; left out when a day is missing), **Edit** (`history.edit`, a 44 × 44 pt target at least) opposite, then the review in the serif italic (two lines), or for an abandoned read its reason.
States: hidden for a Book not in the Library or on Want to read (no reads) · reads · load error with nothing to show (`history.loadError`, quiet line).
Actions → result:
- Edit (`history.edit`) → the Edit sheet for that read (see Edit read sheet).
Data: `sessions(entryId)` (`data/library.ts`), one request over the `reading_sessions` rows of the entry (RLS limits them to the member's own), ordered open first, then by end day, start day and creation (`sortSessions`, the order of the database's `latest_session` for the whole list). Read when the page shows the entry and again whenever the entry changes on this device (a start, finish, abandon, read again, edit, delete); only the latest ask lands, and a failed refresh keeps what is showing.
Edge cases: **one day-counting rule everywhere**, both ends counted (`daysSpanned` in `utils/dates.ts`): the start day is day 1, so the status line, Home, the Library card and the history agree ("day 4" while open; a closed read of 3 Oct to 5 Oct is "3 days"; begun and ended on one day is "1 day", never "Same day"); a read logged without a start or end day shows what it has. Signing out (or another member signing in) forgets the reads.

Flow: no flow; the order in `web/tests/history.test.ts`, the day counting in `web/tests/rating-control-and-days.test.ts`
