### Next in your series  (web: `components/home/{NextInSeries,NextRow,NextSheet,NextMenu}.vue` last on `app/pages/index.vue`, `components/author/WorkRow.vue`, `components/ui/{Reveal,ListMotion,Sheet}.vue`, `stores/series.ts`, `startedOnHome` and `nextPlace` in `utils/enrich.ts`, `started`, `muted`, `mute`, `unmute` in `data/enrich/series.ts`, `moveMuted` in `utils/enrich.ts`; database: `started_series`, `muted_series_list`, `mute_series`, `unmute_series`, table `muted_series`)
Purpose (#167): what is still open in the series she has started, quietly, on Home.
The rule: a series is **started** when at least one of its works is currently reading or finished. Want to read alone does not start one; neither does a work she gave up on (finished, its latest read abandoned: *Not finished*). It is **unfinished** while a work of it is open: not finished, not being read, not given up on. The row shows the **next open work**: the first one after the furthest she has started in it, else the first gap before. The Catalogue's list of the series is all there is to go on, so **an unknown total never makes a series unfinished**: one whose every known work she has finished is complete and not listed (the same when only works she is reading or gave up on are left). Her corrections count (her entry placed in a series at a position); where a sub-series and its parent both qualify only the sub-series is named (City Watch, not Discworld). Order: her latest activity in the series first (the latest finish, or the day she began the work she is reading), then the name.
**Muting** (the whole series, never single books): a series she does not plan to continue is muted from its row's **...** button and leaves the list; muted ones are found again in the sheet (See all n) and unmuted there. A muted series stays muted when she reads another work of it (the mute belongs to the series). Muting is **online only**, like her series corrections: not queued in the outbox (no `sync_write` action, nothing in `QUEUED_ACTIONS`); the repository refuses it offline (`offline`, nothing sent) and the controls say "Offline". A muted series that no longer qualifies as started (every known work finished, being read or given up on) is in neither list; the mute is kept and it shows again, muted, if it qualifies again. When a sub-series and its parent both qualify, muting the sub-series hides it and its parent does not take its place (the parent is still left out); muting the parent hides nothing while the sub-series is the one named.
Layout: Home's last section, only when there is one. The eyebrow `series.next` (a 44 pt row, so nothing moves when the pill comes) and the latest **five** series as rows: small cover of the next open work, its serif title, a mono line "Book 3 of 10 · Discworld" (`series.nextPlaceOf`; "Book 2.5 · Discworld", `series.nextPlace`, where the place is a novella or past the count; the name alone where the series gives no place), and her status of it (Want to read) or the pill + Want to read. With **more than five**, or when she muted some, the eyebrow row ends in a **See all n** pill (`series.seeAll`, n = the series she has open, a chevron — never the muted count; named `series.seeMoreLabel` "See all 7 series you have started"); with five or fewer and none muted there is none. It opens the **sheet** (`UiSheet`, `homeSeries`, modelled on the Read in sheet), titled `series.started`: every started series in the same rows and order, its body scrolling. Each row ends in a **...** button (`series.moreLabel` "More about <series>", `home.nextMenu` / `homeSeries.rowMenu`, a 44 pt target, no swipe: it would fight the sheet's swipe-down and iOS's Back-swipe) that opens the series' **menu** (`UiSheet`, `homeSeriesMenu`, titled with the series' name, one row **Mute series** `series.mute` and the hint `series.muteHint`). At the bottom of the sheet, only when she muted one or more, the row **Muted (n)** (`series.muted`, a chevron that turns): it opens the list of them, each its name and **Unmute** (`series.unmute`, named `series.unmuteLabel` "Unmute <series>"). The pill always opens the sheet with the muted list closed.
States: none (no section, no room kept: it is the page's last block; it also has none while nothing is started and nothing muted) · all muted (the eyebrow and the pill See all 0 stay, no rows, so she can still unmute: the section hides only when there is nothing started and nothing muted) · rows (from the device's copy at once, offline too, refreshed when Home shows) · first answer (a device with no copy: the section opens its room and fades in over `standard` (`UiReveal`); everything above it stays where it is) · a change (she finished or started a Book, here or elsewhere: a row that leaves fades over `exit` and closes its room, one that arrives opens it, the others slide, `UiListMotion`, once no sheet is on screen (`useSettled`); a series whose next work changed is a row leaving and another arriving) · the last row going closes the section like a Reveal.
Actions → result: **...** → the menu (over the sheet too); Mute series → `mute_series` and, once the server has answered, the menu closes and the row leaves the list with its motion (`exit`, the others slide, after the menu has fallen away) and appears under Muted (n); offline the row is disabled and says "Offline" (`common.offline`); a refusal stays in the menu with its reason (`series.error.*`, `homeSeriesMenu.error`) · Muted (n) → opens or closes the muted list · Unmute → `unmute_series`; the series leaves the muted list at once (the sheet's lists follow her while it is open, Home behind it once it has gone) and is back on Home; offline the button says "Offline"; a refusal shows under the list (`homeSeries.error`) · a row → the Book's page; + Want to read → the Add sheet (over the sheet too), as on the author page; See all n → the sheet, a row of it → the Book's page (which closes the sheet).
**Want to read, added in a row** (the animation): when the Add sheet is confirmed, the pill fades away over `exit` and shrinks to 0.97 and her status takes its place in the same cell, so the row is no taller or shorter and nothing moves: after the Add sheet has fallen away (`sheet-exit`) it rises the last `xs` and lights in the accent, then settles to its quiet colour over twice `sheet`, as the tally's new mark does. The pill and the status sit in one grid cell. With Reduce Motion: the status is there at once. The same for any status that takes the pill's place (a Start from another screen), on the author page and the series sheet too (`WorkRow`).
Edge cases: the section lists series whose next work can be opened (an edition to add, or her Book); one with none is left out. A series she is reading the last open work of is not listed (it is a card higher up). The sheet's list does not change while it is open. `started_series` asks for up to 200 (the app asks for 100); `next_in_series` stays in the database for installed apps that still call it. The device's copy has a new shape (`DEVICE_ENRICH_VERSION` 2): the old one is dropped, never misread. Last on Home, so it never moves what is above it.

IDs: `home.nextInSeries`, `home.next`, `home.nextLink`, `home.nextTitle`, `home.nextMeta`, `home.nextStatus`, `home.nextWant`, `home.nextMore`, `homeSeries`, `homeSeries.sheetTitle`, `homeSeries.cancel`, `homeSeries.scrim`, `homeSeries.list`, `homeSeries.row`, `homeSeries.rowLink`, `homeSeries.rowTitle`, `homeSeries.rowMeta`, `homeSeries.rowStatus`, `homeSeries.rowWant`, `home.nextMenu`, `homeSeries.rowMenu`, `homeSeriesMenu` (`.sheetTitle`, `.cancel`, `.scrim`, `.mute`, `.error`), `homeSeries.muted`, `homeSeries.mutedToggle`, `homeSeries.mutedList`, `homeSeries.mutedRow`, `homeSeries.mutedName`, `homeSeries.unmute`, `homeSeries.error`
Flow: no flow (muting is not a critical path); the rule at the database in `supabase/tests/started_series_test.sql` and `supabase/tests/muted_series_test.sql`, the data layer in `web/tests/enrich.test.ts`, what Home lists and how a mute moves a row in `web/tests/enrich-present.test.ts`.
Database (muting): `muted_series(member_id, series_id, muted_at)`, readable by its owner only (RLS), no direct write; `mute_series(p_series)` / `unmute_series(p_series)` (security definer, idempotent, `series_not_found` for a series she cannot see, `not_signed_in` signed out, `authenticated` only). `started_series(p_limit, p_language)` keeps its signature and its JSON (an array of items) and leaves muted series out, so installed apps keep working; `muted_series_list(p_limit, p_language)` answers the muted ones in the same item shape (a second function, not a sibling key: a key would turn the array into an object and break installed apps). Muted or not is decided after the parent/sub-series choice.
Native: Home's last section; a pill; a sheet with a scrolling list; the status change is a quiet cross-fade with a short rise; a "..." per row opening a small menu (a context menu or action sheet; never a swipe), Muted (n) in the sheet.

### Errors, the owner's client error log  (web: `app/pages/profile/errors.vue`, `components/errors/DetailSheet.vue`, the row in `components/profile/Account.vue`, `stores/ownerErrors.ts`, `data/ownerErrors.ts`, `utils/relativeTime.ts`; database: `owner_client_errors`, `owner_client_error_detail`, `private.instance_owner`, `private.is_instance_owner()`)
Purpose: the instance's owner reads what went wrong on devices (docs/OPERATIONS.md, Client errors) in the app instead of the dashboard. The owner and nobody else: the database raises `not_owner` (42501) for any other member and for everyone while `private.instance_owner` is empty, `anon` is not granted the functions, and the app shows the row and the page only for the member named by `NUXT_PUBLIC_SHELF_OWNER_ID` (the same one as Your shelf's, with or without Regal).
Layout — **Profile → Account**: the row **Errors** (flag icon, chevron) after Book links, only for the owner; at its right a small badge with how many groups were first seen in the last 24 hours (`profile.errorsNew`, `ownerErrors.rowNew` as its label), else the number of groups in mono, else `ownerErrors.rowNone`. **Errors page** (pushed, like Ebooks; round back, a round Refresh at the top right): the large title, a line on what it is, the kind filter (pills: All and the kinds there are, each with its number of groups, lit like the Profile's year pills; none with one kind only), then one row per group, newest first: a chip with the kind (`ownerErrors.kind.*`), **New** in the lamp colour when first seen in the last 24 hours, ×times at the right in mono, the message (three lines at most), a mono line "5 minutes ago · build · route". A tap opens the sheet **<kind>**: the full message, the latest stack (mono, scrolls both ways; "Loading the stack…", "That report has no stack.", or "The stack didn't load" with Try again), then the group's figures (times, first seen, last seen, members as a number, signed-out times when there are, builds, routes, installed · in a tab, online · offline, the latest device). **Copy** at the sheet's top right puts "[kind] message / ×times / route · build · device / stack" on the clipboard and reads **Copied** for two seconds.
Data: the last 7 days (the log keeps 30), grouped by kind and message (so the same error in two builds is one group with both), at most 200, newest last seen first; counts are the sum of the reports' `count`; "first seen" is the first time ever within what the log keeps, so an old error that came back is not New. Never another member's id: `members` is a number.
States: loading (the page shows nothing under the title until the first answer) · groups · filtered · empty (D's empty state, `ownerErrors.empty.title` "No errors in the last 7 days" over `ownerErrors.empty.text`) · load failed (`ownerErrors.loadError` with Try again; with groups already on screen a failed refresh keeps them) · offline (Refresh disabled; the load waits and runs when the connection is back). Sheet: stack loading · stack · no stack · stack failed.
Actions → result: Errors row → the page, which loads the groups · Refresh → asks again (the icon turns while it does) · a kind pill → only its groups, All → all of them (a refresh that leaves a kind without groups goes back to All) · a group → its sheet, the stack asked once per group and kept until a newer report · Copy → clipboard (falls back to selecting the text where the browser has no clipboard API) · Cancel, the scrim, Back → the sheet closes.
Edge cases: for anyone but the owner no row, the address `/profile/errors` is the 404 of any unknown address, and no request for the log is ever made; the stores forget everything when the member changes or signs out. A build without `NUXT_PUBLIC_SHELF_OWNER_ID` has the screens for nobody. The database is set separately (docs/OPERATIONS.md, The owner).
Copy keys: `ownerErrors.*`
IDs: `profile.errors`, `profile.errorsNew`, `profile.errorsValue`, `errors.title`, `errors.back`, `errors.refresh`, `errors.kinds`, `errors.kind.<all|kind>`, `errors.list`, `errors.group`, `errors.groupKind`, `errors.groupNew`, `errors.groupTimes`, `errors.groupMessage`, `errors.groupMeta`, `errors.emptyTitle`, `errors.empty`, `errors.loadFailed`, `errors.retry`, `errorDetail`, `errorDetail.action` (Copy), `errorDetail.cancel`, `errorDetail.message`, `errorDetail.stack`, `errorDetail.noStack`, `errorDetail.stackLoading`, `errorDetail.stackError`, `errorDetail.retry`, `errorDetail.times|firstSeen|lastSeen|members|signedOut|versions|routes|installed|online|device`
Flow: no flow; data layer `web/tests/owner-errors.test.ts`; rules `supabase/tests/owner_client_errors_test.sql`.
Native: the same two functions (`owner_client_errors`, `owner_client_error_detail`) and the same owner row; grouping and the New rule are in the database and `data/ownerErrors.ts` (ported 1:1); the stack goes to the system's share sheet or clipboard.

### Waitlist, a visitor leaves an address; the owner reads the list  (web: `components/reading/Waitlist.vue` and `Footer.vue`, `app/pages/profile/waitlist.vue`, the row in `components/profile/Account.vue`, `stores/waitlist.ts`, `data/waitlist.ts`; rules `supabase/migrations/20261011040000_waitlist.sql` and `20261013010000_waitlist_invite.sql`; the mail `supabase/functions/waitlist-invite`)
Purpose (#171): Libellus is invite-only and a reading page creates no account, so where it used to say "ask her for a code" it takes the visitor's address: *"Libellus is invite-only for now. Leave your email and we'll send you an invite when there's room."* The owner invites from her list: **Invite** mails one entry a one-use code (the `waitlist-invite` edge function, over the instance's SMTP) and marks it invited; or she copies the addresses, invites by hand and marks them (**Mark invited**).
Layout — **the form** (end of `/r/<token>` and of a Book card, signed out, above "Have an invite code? Sign up"): heading `readingPage.waitlist.title`, the sentence `…text`, one email field (`…email`, autocomplete `email`, mobile email keyboard, Return sends), **Join the waitlist**, and under it the consent line `…consent` ("We'll only use it to send you an invite; ask us to delete it any time."; there is no privacy page on main yet, so no link — draft sentence in the PR for #102). A honeypot field (`website`) no person reaches (out of the tab order, hidden from assistive tech) goes along. **The owner's list**: Profile → Account → **Waitlist** (owner only; value "N waiting" or "Nobody") → a pushed page: the round back, Refresh in the bar, the title, `waitlist.intro`, "N waiting · M invited" and **Copy waiting emails**, then one block an entry: the address, "3 days ago · from Ada's page" and a trash button behind a Confirm ("Delete <address>? … removed for good. Use this when someone asks to be forgotten."); beneath, **Invite** (waiting entries only) and **Mark invited** (becomes **Invited**, tap again to take it back; an invited address reads muted). **Invite** asks first in a Confirm (`waitlist.inviteConfirm.*`: "Send an invite to <address>?", what she gets, **Send invite**, Cancel); the mail she gets is Libellus' designed mail (the sign-in code's shell, light and dark: "You're invited", the code in the code cell, until when, how to use it, a **Sign up** button only when the instance has `LIBELLUS_SITE_URL`; source `design/emails/invite.mjs`); once answered the entry shows its code in mono with **Copy code** and a polite line under it: "Emailed. Works once, until <date>." (the entry is then **Invited**), or, when nothing went out, why (`waitlist.code.not_configured`: mail isn't set up; `…send_failed`: the email could not be sent) with "send the code another way" — the entry stays waiting, Invite again gets the same code. The codes are kept for the session only (never on the device).
States — form: ready; sending ("Joining…"); **"You're on the list"** (polite status replacing the form, the same for an address that was already there); an address that is not one (`…invalid`, under the field, `aria-invalid`, nothing sent); too many tries (`…rateLimited`, `role=alert`); could not be added (`…error`, the button reads **Try again**, the address stays); offline (the button says Offline). List: loading; entries; nobody (D's empty state); load failed (why and **Try again**, a failed refresh keeps the entries); a write refused (`waitlist.writeError`); offline (writes disabled, "Offline"). Invite: the Confirm busy while it asks (Invite and Mark invited of that entry disabled); refused (not the owner, the function missing or failing: `waitlist.inviteError` in the Confirm, **Send invite** tries again; offline: `waitlist.offline`); mailed (code, "Emailed", entry invited); not mailed (code, why, entry waiting); mailed but the marking failed (the app marks it the manual way; `waitlist.writeError` when that fails too).
Rules (database): `join_waitlist(email, token, website)` trims and lower-cases, refuses a shape that is not an address (`email_invalid`), 5 new entries an hour per salted caller key and 100 in all (`rate_limited`, checked before the list is looked at, so a refusal says nothing about an address), the same address again answers the same and changes nothing, the token only finds the page's member (`source_member_id`; never stored), a dead token joins from no member, a filled honeypot answers the same and stores nothing; `consent_text_version` is the wording's version. The table is in `private`: no member reads it. `owner_waitlist`, `owner_waitlist_set_invited(ids, on)`, `owner_waitlist_delete(id)`, `owner_waitlist_prepare_invite(id)` raise `not_owner` (42501) for anyone but the member in `private.instance_owner`; `anon` is not granted them. `owner_waitlist_prepare_invite(id)` answers the address, a one-use code valid 14 days and its expiry: the entry's own code (`private.waitlist.invite_code_id`) while it is unused and unexpired, else a new one (label `waitlist`, never the address); an unknown id raises `waitlist_entry_not_found`; it never marks the entry. The function `waitlist-invite` (`verify_jwt` on) calls it with the owner's own session (no service-role key), mails, and only then calls `owner_waitlist_set_invited`; without SMTP secrets or on a failed send it answers the code with `emailed: false` and `reason` (`not_configured`, `send_failed`), 403 for `not_owner`; it never logs the address or the code. The app shows the row and the page only for the member named by `NUXT_PUBLIC_SHELF_OWNER_ID` (as Errors), the address `/profile/waitlist` is a 404 for anyone else and nothing is requested for them.
Copy keys: `readingPage.waitlist.*`, `readingPage.footer.signUp`, `waitlist.*`

Flow: `web/e2e/reading-page.spec.ts` (signed out: joins, the same address again, an address that is not one, no account made; the dark scan; the limit and offline: `web/tests/waitlist.test.ts`); the owner's list and invites have no flow; data layer `web/tests/waitlist.test.ts`; rules `supabase/tests/waitlist_test.sql`, `supabase/tests/waitlist_invite_test.sql`; the function `supabase/functions/waitlist-invite/*_test.ts`.

### Share target and app shortcuts  (web: `app/pages/share.vue`, `stores/share.ts`, `data/shareResolve.ts`, `utils/shared.ts`, `utils/pendingShare.ts`, `utils/launch.ts`, `composables/useLaunch.ts` used by `layouts/tabs.vue`, the keep-through-sign-in in `middleware/auth.global.ts`; the manifest's `share_target` and `shortcuts` in `nuxt.config.ts`; icons `design/icons/app/shortcuts/*.svg` rendered to `public/shortcut-*-96.png` by `scripts/render-icons.mjs`)
Purpose (#91): sharing a link or text to Libellus from Goodreads, Amazon, a browser or a bookstore app (Android's share sheet) opens Libellus on the Book; long-pressing the app icon offers Search, Update progress and Library.
Layout: `/share` has no chrome: the wordmark "libellus" in the serif italic and "Finding your book…" under it, centred, for as long as the lookup takes. Nothing else; it never stays on screen.
Share, in: the manifest's `share_target`, a `POST` with `multipart/form-data` since #131 (files need it, and a manifest has one share target). The service worker answers it (`public/sw-share.js`): a share with only `title`, `text` or `url` is redirected (303) to `/share?title=&text=&url=`, the address #91 read as a GET, so everything below is unchanged; EPUB files go to *Ebooks on the device*. Apps fill the three as they please (Chrome puts the link in `text` and the page title in `title`), so all three are read together.
Reading a share (`parseShared`, pure, `web/tests/shared.test.ts`), in order: (1) an **ISBN** wherever it stands, always with its check digit validated, an ISBN-10 converted to ISBN-13: an Amazon product address (`/dp/<ISBN-10>`, `/gp/product/<ISBN-10>`, `/gp/aw/d/`, `/exec/obidos/ASIN/`, any Amazon domain; an ASIN like `B0…` is no ISBN), an `isbn=`/`isbn13=`/`ean=`/`gtin=` parameter, any ISBN-13 in a link or the text (hyphens allowed); an ISBN-10 in other places only where it is plausible (hyphenated, labelled "ISBN", or standing alone as a path segment or parameter value), because one in eleven ten-digit numbers passes the check. (2) A **Goodreads** book link (`/book/show/<id>`) gives the id. (3) The words to search: the shared sentence, else the page title, with links, ISBNs and boilerplate ("Check out", "on Goodreads", "| Goodreads", "Amazon.com: … : Books") removed and "Title by Author" turned into "Title Author" (a search matches words); a Goodreads link's slug if nothing else names the Book.
Finding the Book (`resolveShare`, `web/tests/share-resolve.test.ts`): an ISBN → the same search as the palette (Catalogue, Apple Books, OpenLibrary, merged; the first hit). No ISBN but a Goodreads id → the ISBN the shared Goodreads cache (`goodreads_ratings`) holds for that id, then the same lookup. Still nothing and a Goodreads id → a text search, taking the hit whose title is the shared title (accents, case and a subtitle ignored). A bare title is never taken straight to a Book (editions and namesakes are her pick).
Actions → result:
- A Book is found → `/book/<key>`, **replacing** `/share` in the history (Back never lands on a page that only redirects; with nothing before it, the book page's own back goes to the Library).
- No Book is certain (nothing found, offline, a source down, only a title) → Home with the search palette open and the words typed in (`/?search=1&q=<words>`, handled by the shell, replacing `/share`); offline the palette searches the Library as always.
- Nothing usable shared → Home.
- Signed out → the share is kept on the device (`libellus.pendingShare`, one hour, cleared by sign-out like all `libellus.` data) and the member is sent to sign in; after the code, whatever screen the sign-in leads to, she lands on `/share` and from there on the Book. A share in the address replaces one kept before.
Shortcuts (manifest `shortcuts`, 96 px icons from the ribbons set: a magnifier whose lens holds the lamp, the shorter ribbon with the lamp, the two ribbons): **Search** → `/?search=1` opens the palette over Home; **Update progress** → `/?progress=1` asks the Library again (another device may have changed it) and opens the book page of the Currently reading entry whose progress was updated last (a read never updated counts from its start) with the Update progress sheet open, Home underneath so Back leads to it; Home stays as it is when nothing is being read; **Library** → `/library`. The shell takes `search`, `progress` and `q` off the address once it has acted on them.
Copy keys: `share.title`, `share.finding`
IDs: `share.brand`, `share.status`
Flow: no flow; `web/tests/shared.test.ts` (an ISBN in a shared link, an Amazon `/dp/` and `/gp/product/` ISBN-10, a Goodreads link, shared title text, the share kept through the sign-in, the Update progress shortcut's pick) and `web/tests/share-resolve.test.ts` (where a share goes: the book, the palette with the shared words, an ISBN no source knows). The text share POSTed through the service worker is not tested end to end. On a device: see `docs/TESTING.md` (the share sheet and long-press need a WebAPK).
Native: Android — an `ACTION_SEND` (`text/plain`) intent filter that runs the same reader over `EXTRA_SUBJECT`/`EXTRA_TEXT` (the grammar above, the same tests), the same lookup; static or dynamic app shortcuts for the three actions. iOS — a Share Extension that accepts URLs and text and hands them to the same reader; Home Screen quick actions for the three shortcuts.

### Ebooks on the device  (web: `app/pages/ebooks.vue`, `components/ebooks/{WaitingRow,CandidateSheet,EditionChoiceSheet,CopySheet,LinkNote,Mark}.vue`, her own group and linking in `components/search/{Results,ResultRow}.vue`, `components/book/Ebook.vue` and the ebook rows of `components/book/OptionsSheet.vue` in `app/pages/book/[key].vue`, the Ebooks row of `components/profile/Account.vue`, `stores/ebooks.ts`, `data/ebooks/{epub,match,files,ingest,ebooks,worker,snapshot}.ts`, `utils/ebookWorker.ts`, `clearLocalFiles`, the `ebooks`/`ebookFolders` stores and the `libellus.ebooks` snapshot under `data/localData.ts`'s prefix; the share target in `public/sw-share.js`, `app/pages/share.vue`, `functions/share.js` (Cloudflare Pages); no database change)
Purpose (#131, phase 1): EPUB files the member has on her device are linked to Books in her Library, from three places: the share sheet (one or many files), the ebook folder (picked once, scanned on a tap) and the file picker on a Book page. Nothing is ever uploaded: every linked file is **copied into the origin private file system** (OPFS), so reading it later (phase 2) needs no permission and works offline; the folder handle only finds files (phase 0, decided by the owner).
Taking a file in (`ingestEbook`, in a worker with fflate): the package document (`META-INF/container.xml` → OPF; its text decoded by its byte order mark, else the encoding its XML declaration names, else strictly as UTF-8 with Windows-1252 for bytes that are not, and composed to NFC, `decodeXml`, so a "u" with a combining diaeresis is "ü") gives the title (EPUB 3's `title-type` main, else the first), the authors (`dc:creator` without editors, translators, illustrators by `opf:role` or EPUB 3's `role` refinement; "Last, First" turned round), language, publisher, every ISBN (`urn:isbn:`, `isbn:`, an identifier labelled ISBN by `opf:scheme` or `identifier-type` 15/02, or a bare valid ISBN-13; ISBN-10 converted) and the cover image (EPUB 3 `cover-image`, EPUB 2 `<meta name="cover">`, else an image item called cover). The copy's name is its fingerprint: SHA-256 of the first megabyte plus the size (`ebooks/<member id>/<hash32>-<size>.epub`, the cover beside it as `.cover`). The same bytes are one copy and one record, whatever they were called or wherever they came from. Not an EPUB → `not_epub`; the copy could not be written → `storage`.
The record (IndexedDB `libellus` → `ebooks`, keyed by member and id): OPFS path and cover path, original name, size, last change (null for a shared file: the share sheet gives the time it was received), the path inside the picked folder, the fingerprint, the metadata, the state (*linked* to a Book and its entry, *unlinked* with its candidate Books, *ignored*), when it was linked and added, and its source (`folder` | `share` | `picker`). The picked folder's handle is in `ebookFolders` (structured-cloned; a handle that cannot be stored lives for the session). `navigator.storage.persist()` is asked once, after the first link. One record per file identity (the fingerprint): a renamed or moved file is the same file, with its outcome — linked to an entry, ignored (*Ignore*, *Keep current*) or replaced by another copy (`ignoredAs: 'replaced'`: its copy deleted, the record kept) — so no scan or share offers a decided file again. `reader` says which reading of the package document its metadata comes from (`EPUB_READER_VERSION`, now 2).
Matching (`matchEbook`, pure, `web/tests/ebooks.test.ts`) reads the file's title cleaned (`cleanTitle`: NFC, invisible format characters such as a soft hyphen or zero-width space dropped — `normalise` drops them too, never reading them as a space —, underscores as spaces, no `.epub`, no trailing brackets with a year, a counter, a series number or a download site's name: "Harry Potter und der Halbblutprinz (2005)(1)" → "Harry Potter und der Halbblutprinz", "(The Forever War, #1)", "(Sun Eater Book 6)", "[Retail]"; an author written before it, "Haldeman, Joe - The Forever War"; a series in brackets before it, "[Sun Eater 06] Disquiet Gods") and its authors composed, against the member's Library entries (read again first when online): (1) an ISBN of the file is the ISBN-13 (or converted ISBN-10) of an entry's Book → linked; (2) the titles are the same as search's matching reads them (accents, case, punctuation aside), whole or without subtitles (`:`, `;`, `(`, `[`, " - ", ", or"), or the same letters spaced differently, and the Book's first author is one of the file's (same surname, or every word of one in the other) → linked; (3) several entries fit, or a title fits without its author → waits with those entries as candidates; (4) nothing → waits, *Not in your Library yet*. One file per Book: a match to a Book that has another file linked waits for the member (never replaced on its own); linking by hand replaces the old file (its copy is deleted). A waiting file is tried again after every add from the Add sheet (after an optimistic add from search, once it has synced).
Layout and states:
- **Book page**: under the action, when a file is linked, one quiet line: the ebook icon and *Ebook · on this device*; *Ebook · missing* (error colour) with *Share or scan the file again to bring the copy back.* when the copy is gone (OPFS evicted). While a picked file is taken in: *Adding the ebook…*; a refusal: `ebooks.error.not_epub` / `.storage`. With the copy here, *Read now* (see *Reader*).
- **Options sheet** (Book in the Library, where the browser has OPFS): *Add ebook* (no file) or *Replace ebook file*, the native file input (`accept=".epub,application/epub+zip"`) over the row, so the tap opens the picker; the sheet closes once a file is chosen. *Unlink ebook* → a confirmation (*Remove the ebook from this device?*, the file's name; *Remove ebook*) → copy, cover and record deleted, the Book stays. A picked file that is clearly another book (none of its ISBNs is the Book's while it has ISBNs both sides, or its title is not the Book's) → the sheet *Another book?* with the file's title and author; *Link* links it anyway, Cancel drops it (nothing kept).
- **Library rows and cards, Home's reading cards**: the ebook icon (13 px, faint ink) after the author for a Book with a linked file (`library.ebookMark`, `home.ebookMark`).
- **Profile → Account**: one row, *Ebooks* (where OPFS exists) → the Ebooks page, its value *N need you* or the number linked. The folder is chosen and scanned on the Ebooks page only (owner, 2026-10: the Account keeps no Ebook folder row and no Scan).
- **Ebooks page**: the report of the last share or scan, a raised card (`p-inset`, `pt-ms`): the eyebrow (*Shared* / *Scanned*) and Dismiss (the quiet text action, `UiButton` plain sm, centred on the eyebrow's line, its 44 px target reaching outside the card's padding) on one line, then `{n} ebooks · {n} linked · {n} need you · {n} other copies` and *1 isn't an EPUB*; *missed* (a share whose files were not kept: share again); the folder group (name, *Scan* the first time and *Scan again* once the folder was scanned → "Scanning… 3 of 12"; under it, quietly, *Scanned 2 minutes ago* from the folder's own last scan time, and when that scan is from before this app session or older than about a day *· Added books to your folder? Scan again.* — never a scan or a permission prompt unasked; the error *Libellus could not read the folder. Tap Scan and allow it, or choose the folder again.*, the hint about the Download folder); *Needs you*: each waiting file with its own cover (`UiCover` `whole`: a cover clearly off 2:3 is fitted whole into the slot over a blurred copy of itself, never cut at the sides), its cleaned title, author, file name in mono, *Could be one of N of your books* or *Not in your Library yet*, and *Choose book* (only with candidates: a sheet with the file and the candidates' covers; a tap links), *Find book*, *Ignore*; *Other copies*: a waiting file whose one fitting Book has an ebook already, apart and quieter, *Another copy of a linked book* with *Replace* (the sheet below) and *Keep current* (the file is ignored); *Linked*: each linked file's Book (cover, title, file name; *Missing* when evicted) opening the book page; the empty state where no folder can be picked and nothing was shared; the footer *Ebook files stay on this device…*. **First frame**: the page renders from the device's snapshot (`libellus.ebooks`, `data/ebooks/snapshot.ts`: the records' display data — never file contents —, which copies were missing, the folder's name and last scan), read synchronously when the store is set up and written after every read of the records; IndexedDB then reconciles it in place (same order, same keys), and covers fade in over their slot. Without a snapshot (the first visit) the folder value, the Scan button and three rows are loading placeholders (the Profile's, #123). Measured on a reload (Chromium, CPU 6× slower, 12 files): CLS 0.036 before, 0 after.
- **Sheets of Find book**: **Which edition?** (`ebookEdition`, from a result marked *Other edition in your Library*): the file (its own cover whole, title, author), a hint, then two radio rows like Change edition's — *Your edition* (picked when it opens: *Link to your edition*) and *Found in search* (*Switch to this edition and link*: her entry changes edition, #41, then the file is linked to it; a refusal shows on the row); the action *Link*. **<title> already has an ebook** (`ebookCopy`, a tap on an entry that has a file, or *Replace* on an *Other copies* row): the file, *This file* and *Linked* file names in mono, *Replace with this file* (*The current copy is removed from this device.*) and *Keep the current one* (this file is ignored). After a link from search, a note floats above the tab bar for 5 s: *Linked to <title>* (opens the book page) and *Undo* (the file waits again as it did; none after a replace).
Actions → result:
- **Share** one or many EPUBs to the installed app → the service worker keeps them in Cache Storage (`libellus-shared-ebooks`, `/__shared/<id>/<n>` and `/meta`) and redirects (303) to `/share?ebooks=<id>` → *Taking in your ebooks… 2 of 3* → every waiting share is taken in and its entries removed from the cache (one that arrives meanwhile, or one that could not be taken in, stays for the next time) → the Ebooks page with the report, replacing `/share` in the history. Signed out → kept through the sign-in like a text share (`libellus.pendingShare` carries `ebooks`). A POST that reaches the host without a service worker (the first share right after installing) → `functions/share.js` redirects to `/share?ebooks=missed`, as the service worker does when the device has no room to keep the files → the Ebooks page says *The shared files did not reach Libellus. Share them again.*
- **Scan** (a tap) → the folder's permission is queried; when Android forgot it (every cold start, reload or kill: phase 0) `requestPermission` asks on this tap (Chrome's *Allow* dialog), never unasked; refused → the folder error. Then every `.epub` under the folder (subfolders too), its size and last change looked at without reading it: a known path with the same size and last change whose copy is there is skipped unread; an unknown path with a known file's name, size and last change is that file, moved; anything else is read and fingerprinted (an evicted copy is written again, a renamed file is found, a file saved over a known one is taken in). A known file skipped unread keeps its outcome; one read with an older reading of the package document is read again from its copy, a waiting one is matched again (a fix to reading or matching reaches it), and a linked one whose entry left the Library waits again. Never a scan unasked: not on opening the app either (owner, #131).
- **Find book** → the search palette opens over the Ebooks page, linking (`search.linking`, see Search), with the file's cleaned title (without subtitle) and first author; her own Books come first (*In your Library*, also by the file's author), a tap on one links the file to it at once (Undo in the note), an entry with an ebook already asks Replace / Keep, an *Other edition in your Library* result asks *Which edition?*, and the next Book she adds from the Add sheet within 15 minutes is linked to the file. Coming back to the Ebooks page ends it.
- **Replace** (another copy) → this file is linked to the Book; the one linked before is ignored as replaced (its copy deleted, its record kept). **Keep current** → this file is ignored. Neither comes back on a scan.
- **Ignore** → the copy is deleted, the record stays *ignored*: the same file shared or scanned again is counted and left alone.
- A change of edition (#41) → the record follows its entry to the new Book.
- **Sign out** (and Delete account) → the `libellus` database (records, folder handle) and OPFS `ebooks/` are deleted, and the shared-files cache.
Edge cases: no OPFS (old browsers, an insecure context) → nothing ebook-related is offered. Offline: linking, unlinking, scanning and sharing all work (device only); Find book searches the Library as always. A file without an OPF → `not_epub`. Two copies of the same book with different bytes are two files; only one can be linked to the Book. A title is never cut at ". " ("Dr. Jekyll and Mr. Hyde" is not "Dr. No"). Shares and scans are taken in one batch at a time.

IDs: `book.ebook` (`data-missing`), `book.ebookMissingHint`, `book.ebookAdding`, `book.ebookError`, `bookOptions.ebookRow`, `bookOptions.addEbook`, `bookOptions.replaceEbook`, `bookOptions.unlinkEbook`, `unlinkEbook` (+ `.confirm`, `.cancel`), `ebookAnother` (+ `.action`, `.cancel`, `.text`), `library.ebookMark`, `home.ebookMark`, `profile.ebooks`, `profile.ebooksValue`, `ebooks.title`, `ebooks.back`, `ebooks.missed`, `ebooks.report`, `ebooks.reportLine`, `ebooks.reportFailed`, `ebooks.reportDismiss`, `ebooks.folderSection`, `ebooks.folder`, `ebooks.folderName`, `ebooks.scan`, `ebooks.scannedAt`, `ebooks.scanHint`, `ebooks.loading`, `ebooks.folderError`, `ebooks.needsYou`, `ebooks.waiting`, `ebooks.waitingTitle`, `ebooks.waitingFile`, `ebooks.waitingWhy`, `ebooks.choose`, `ebooks.find`, `ebooks.ignore`, `ebooks.copies`, `ebooks.copy`, `ebooks.copyReplace`, `ebooks.copyKeep`, `ebooks.linkNote`, `ebooks.linkNoteBook`, `ebooks.linkUndo`, `ebookEdition` (+ `.mine`, `.switch`, `.action`, `.error`), `ebookCopy` (+ `.file`, `.current`, `.replace`, `.keep`), `search.own`, `ebooks.linkedSection`, `ebooks.linkedRow`, `ebooks.linkedTitle`, `ebooks.linkedMissing`, `ebooks.footer`, `ebookCandidates` (+ `.candidate`)

Android — an `ACTION_SEND`/`ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE` intent filter for `application/epub+zip`, `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE` with a persisted URI permission for the folder (no tap per session), `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` for Add ebook. iOS — a Share Extension for EPUB files, `UIDocumentPickerViewController` for a file or a folder (security-scoped bookmarks).

### Reader  (web: `components/reader/{Reader,ChromePrinted,ChromeClassic,ChromeScroll,Scrub,TypeSheet,ContentsSheet,SelectionMenu,TranslateSheet,DefineSheet,Search,EndOfBook}.vue`, `reader/engine.ts` over the vendored `reader/foliate-js/`, `stores/reader.ts`, `data/reader/{settings,progress,device,lookup,markup}.ts`, `data/readerPlaces.ts`, `data/readerHighlights.ts`, `utils/{readerChrome,readerFlight}.ts`, `components/ui/{Segmented,Switch,SwitchRow}.vue`, Read now in `app/pages/book/[key].vue` and `components/book/Ebook.vue`; database: `reader_places`, `save_reader_place`, `reader_highlights`, `save_reader_highlight`)
Purpose (#131, phase 2): read the EPUB linked to a Book on this device (phase 1) in Libellus itself, with progress written by itself.
Layout: full screen over the Book page (a modal layer; the app behind is inert). The book's room is sepia by default (light or dark in Aa). Two styles, a device setting: **Printed** (default) — the page alone, a folio in mono at its foot; a tap in the middle floats a glass capsule: Back, *15 % · 103 left* (a tap opens the page slider grown out of the capsule: chapter ticks, the saved progress as a faint tick, a mono label, *p. 9* to go back to where it opened, Done), Search, Contents, Aa. On a wide screen two pages face each other. **Classic** — a tap shows round buttons at the top (Back, the title, Search, Contents) and a bar at the bottom (the progress line as a slider, the chapter, *p. 17 of 120 · 1 min left in chapter*, Aa). Either reads in **Pages** or **Scroll** (a chapter is one long page: a lamp hairline along the top edge for the chapter, the chapter floating under it, bars on scrolling up, *Next chapter* at a chapter's end, pulling on past it turns too). When the progress saved is clearly ahead of the page shown: *Your progress is at p. 40. · Set to p. 12*.
States: opening (the cover flies in) · reading · chrome shown · a sheet open · selection (bubble) · end of the book · failed (*This book could not be opened.*) · missing (*The copy of this book on this device is gone…*).
Actions → result:
- **Read now** (lit, on Want to read and Reading with the copy here; Start reading, or Update progress, steps back under it, quiet; on Finished and Not finished: *Read* in the ebook line) → the reader opens at the newer of the device's place and the server's (its CFI for the same file, else its fraction; at most 800 ms waiting for the server), else the start.
- Turn: a tap at the right / left 30 %, a swipe (Classic), the keys (→, Space, PageDown / ←, PageUp); a turn asked for while one settles is made after it.
- **Progress** (Reading only): once she reads on (a turn or a scroll; not on opening, nor after Contents, Search or the slider until she reads on) the place becomes progress — a page of the Book's page count (or a percent), forward only — written after 10 s on a place, at most once a minute, and at once on closing, through `updateProgress` (the outbox while offline). A Want to read Book: the Start sheet first; until she starts nothing is written.
- **The place**: kept on the device on every page (`libellus.reader.place.<member>.<entry>`) and sent to the server 5 s after the last turn and on closing (`save_reader_place`, never moving back in time).
- **Highlights** (issue #131; table `reader_highlights`, hers alone by RLS): the device keeps the list per entry (`libellus.reader.highlights.<member>.<entry>`: id, entry, the fingerprint of the file it was made in, CFI range and section, one of the four colours — `lamp|sage|sky|rose` —, the selected words cut at 1000 characters, an optional note (none yet), `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `deletedAt`, and `sent`). A highlight made, recoloured or removed is drawn at once and kept on the device, then handed to the **outbox** as the `save_reader_highlight` write (`sync_write` action; `data/readerHighlights.ts`, `highlightWrite`), so it works offline and is applied once; it names no Library entry to show, so a flush of only highlights does not read the Library again. The server's list for the entry (tombstones included) is read when the reader opens (the book waits at most 800 ms for it, like the place) and again every minute while the page is visible, when the app comes back to the foreground and when the connection returns, and merged: **last write wins per highlight id** (`updatedAt` is when the change was made on the device; the database ignores an older one and clamps a clock more than a minute ahead), and a removal is a **tombstone** (`deletedAt`, no words kept: the database empties the excerpt and note) that beats an older edit; an edit made after it brings the highlight back. A highlight that arrives or goes while the book is open is drawn or lifted in place. **The copy:** a CFI only means something in the file it was taken in, so every highlight carries the file's fingerprint (the one `reader_places` keeps). A device whose copy has the same fingerprint draws it; if the file differs (another edition, or after Change edition) the highlight is kept and listed under **Highlights from another copy** at the foot of the Contents sheet (`readerContents.otherCopy`, `.otherCopy.item`, `.otherCopy.remove`; the words, the colour, a close button; copy `reader.contentsSheet.otherCopy.*`), never placed; removing it there writes the tombstone. They belong to the entry, not the file. **Migration:** highlights from before the sync (device-only, no id) get an id and the copy open now on the next reader open and go up once. Sign out and Delete account clear the device copy (`libellus.` keys); deleting the account removes the rows (cascade). Offline highlighting is not disabled: it waits (the sync chip counts it, *Highlight* in the sync sheet).
- **Selection** (a long press on touch screens, the reader's own: a lamp wash and two handles; a mouse selects as usual) → a bubble above the words: Translate (a sheet: the words, the target language a tap away, the translation; the device's translator first, else MyMemory), Define (Wiktionary: parts of speech, senses, an example; for a word or two), Copy (*Copied*), Search (the palette with the words), four highlight colours; a tap on a highlight → its colours and Remove (also on words selected over one). Highlights are kept on the device first and on the server (see **Highlights** below).
- **Aa** → Pages | Scroll; Light, Dark, Sepia; Newsreader or Geist; seven sizes; four margins (Edge to edge … Wide); four line spacings; Justify (one switch: even left and right edges and words split at line ends, `hyphens: auto` by the book's language, for Pages, Scroll and the wide two-column page alike; off: ragged right, nothing split; a line under the row says which is on: `reader.type.justifyOn` / `justifyOff`); Keep the screen on (a wake lock while open); Classic mode (only here: the Profile has no reader row). Applied as they change; kept on the device (`libellus-reader`, kept through sign-out).
- **Contents** → the Book, where she is, the progress line as a slider, the chapters (the current one lit, earlier ones muted, each with its page); a tap goes there.
- **Search** → the app's palette; places in the book (the next one by the query, those before after *From the beginning*), each marked in the page; a tap goes there.
- The last page, turned on → **The end**: the cover, the Book, *Finish* (the Finish sheet) and *Back to the last page*; once finished, *Finished* and *Close the book*.
- Back (the button, Escape, Android's Back) → the chrome, the slider, the bubble or the end page first; then the book closes (the cover flies home).
Edge cases: the book's scripts never run and its pages reach nothing outside the book: every page, SVG and stylesheet is sanitized before it is shown (DOMPurify's allowlist: no `script`, frames, objects, forms, `base`, `meta http-equiv`, `on…` handlers, `javascript:`/`vbscript:`/`data:` URLs; `<link>` and CSS `url()` only to the book's own files; its script files never load), every (X)HTML page carries its own `script-src 'none'` policy, and the page frames run no script at all where the browser still sends them events (Chromium, Firefox; WebKit keeps `allow-scripts`, bug 218086); only an EPUB opens; offline: reading, the place on the device and progress (outbox) work; Translate and Define say they need a connection; MyMemory's daily quota → *The free translations for today are used up.*; another file linked to the Book → the place by fraction; Reduce Motion: no flight, no dip.
Copy keys: `book.read`, `reader.*`

### Barcode scanner  (web: the camera button in the query row of `components/shell/SearchOverlay.vue`, `components/shell/BarcodeScanner.vue`, `composables/useBarcodeScanner.ts`, `utils/barcode.ts`, `utils/barcodeReader.ts`, `utils/barcodeWasm.ts`, `findByIsbn` in `stores/search.ts`, `tick` in `utils/haptics.ts`, the `camera` and `torch` icons)
Purpose (#92): find a book by scanning the barcode on its back instead of typing the ISBN.
Rules: (1) **The button.** A camera button sits in the search overlay's query row while the query is empty (Clear takes its place once there is text). It shows wherever the browser has a camera API and a reader for the barcode: the browser's own `BarcodeDetector` that can read `ean_13` (Chrome on Android), or, everywhere else (iOS Safari and the installed iOS app, Firefox), a WebAssembly decoder (zxing-cpp through `zxing-wasm`). It is hidden only without `getUserMedia`. (2) **One interface, two readers.** `BarcodeReader` (`read(frame)` → codes) has a native and a WebAssembly implementation; everything above it (the loop, the ISBN rules, the view) is the same. The native one asks `BarcodeDetector` for `ean_13`, `code_128` and `code_39` (those it supports) and looks four times a second; the WebAssembly one draws the video frame to a canvas (longest side 960 px), decodes its pixels and looks three times a second. The decoder (a 36 KB script chunk and a 0.9 MB module, served from the app's own origin, not a CDN) is fetched when the scanner opens, in parallel with the permission prompt: it is not in the main bundle and not in the service worker's precache (it is cached as it is used). Where it cannot be fetched (offline the first time) the scanner says "No camera found" (its text names the reader). (3) **The scan.** The scanner is full screen and always night (the dark tokens in either theme): the back camera (`facingMode: environment`) behind a framing guide, a close button, a torch button where the video track has a `torch` capability (never on iOS), one detection at a time. A code that is a book's ends the scan: an EAN-13 starting 978 or 979 whose check digit adds up is the ISBN-13; an ISBN-10 (a Code 128 / Code 39 label) with a valid check digit is converted (`isbnFromBarcode`); anything else (a grocery EAN, 977 magazines, a misread) is ignored, and the view says "That doesn't look like a book's barcode." (4) **Found.** One haptic tick (`tick()`: vibration on Android; on iOS the switch trick, which only plays inside a tap and is otherwise a silent no-op), the view says "Looking up <ISBN>…", and the ISBN is looked up like a typed one (`findByIsbn`: Catalogue, Library, Apple Books, OpenLibrary): a Book found opens `/book/<key>` (and the scanner and the search close); nothing found, a failing lookup or no connection closes the scanner and puts the ISBN in the search's query (search says "No books found" and offers adding it by hand; offline it searches her own Library). (5) **States.** Asking (the permission prompt is up, or the decoder is loading), scanning, **denied** (explains where to allow it, a Close button), **no camera** (none, one that will not start, or no decoder), **offline** (a note in the footer: only the Library can be searched; scanning itself still works). (6) **Closing.** ✕, Escape, the system Back (`useBackDismiss`: it closes the scanner and leaves the search), a change of page, or the app going to the background: and then every camera track is stopped (also when the permission prompt is answered after the scanner closed). Reduce Motion: the scan line holds still in the middle of the frame.
Copy keys: `search.scan.open`, `title`, `close`, `torchOn`, `torchOff`, `hint`, `notBook`, `looking`, `askingTitle`, `asking`, `deniedTitle`, `denied`, `noneTitle`, `none`, `dismiss`, `offline`
IDs: `search.scan` (the camera button), `scan.overlay`, `scan.video`, `scan.frame`, `scan.close`, `scan.torch`, `scan.hint`, `scan.notBook`, `scan.looking`, `scan.asking`, `scan.blocked`, `scan.denied`, `scan.none`, `scan.dismiss`, `scan.offline`
Flow: `web/tests/barcode.test.ts` (EAN-13 and ISBN-10 → ISBN-13 incl. the check digits and what is no book; the loop with fake readers and timers), `web/tests/barcode-reader.test.ts` (both readers: the WebAssembly one on fixture images (`web/tests/fixtures/barcode`: the bars of an ISBN-13, a small turned noisy soft camera frame, a grocery EAN) and the native one with a fake `BarcodeDetector`). No Playwright flow: the camera states (denied, no camera, offline, closing) are not a critical path.
Real devices (docs/TESTING.md, Barcode scanner): `web/e2e/android/scan.ts` on the Android emulator (Chrome's real `BarcodeDetector` lists `ean_13`; it reads the picture of a barcode; the real permission prompt; the scanner on real `getUserMedia` frames of the emulator's test scene; Back closes it) and `web/e2e/ios/scan-harness.ts` on the iOS Simulator (Safari 27: no `BarcodeDetector`, the WebAssembly decoder reads a stand-in camera stream, the book page opens; denied and no-camera states; with the Simulator's own `getUserMedia`, which has no camera and never answers, the view stays on "Allow the camera").
Native: Android — the platform's barcode scanner (ML Kit / CameraX) with the same rules (ISBN-13 starting 978/979 or an ISBN-10, check digit, lookup, then the book page or the search with the ISBN); iOS — VisionKit `DataScannerViewController` or AVFoundation's `AVCaptureMetadataOutput` for EAN-13. The torch and the permission states are the system's.

### Device edges  (no screen of its own; web: `--bar-top`, `--float-bottom` and their utilities in `app/assets/css/main.css`, tokens `barTop`, `floatAbove`, `tabBarDrop`; `components/ui/TopBar.vue`, `components/shell/{Header,TabBar}.vue`, the scroll edge in `layouts/tabs.vue`)
Purpose (#58): the chrome against the device's edges in a browser tab and in the installed app, on Android and on iOS — what the desktop-browser runs could not see.
Rules: (1) **Top.** A bar's 44 pt controls row (the tab header, a pushed screen's top bar) starts at `max(top inset, barTop 8)`: under the status bar in the installed iOS app (59 pt, unchanged), and 8 off the top edge in every browser tab, where the inset is 0, so the round buttons never touch the browser's toolbar. (2) **Bottom, outside iOS.** The tab bar and the resting search palette float `floatAbove` (16) above the bottom inset, as Material 3 floats its toolbar: Android's navigation fills its inset, so nothing may reach into it. Chrome on a Pixel with gesture navigation reports 24 → 40 off the screen edge; three-button navigation and the installed Android app report 0 (the page ends above the bar) → 16. (3) **Bottom, on iOS** (WebKit on iPhone/iPad, told apart by `-webkit-touch-callout`): `tabBarDrop` (13) into the home-indicator inset, `ms` minimum: 21 pt off the edge on a Face ID iPhone, as before. (4) A tab's room under its content (`clear-tab-bar`) is the capsule's top edge and `xxl` — both follow `--float-bottom`.
IDs: none new.
Flow: no Playwright flow; the real devices: `web/e2e/android/smoke.ts` on the Android emulator (docs/TESTING.md).
Native: Android — a floating bottom bar sits 16 dp above `WindowInsets.navigationBars`, the top bar's row under `statusBars` (a native app always has one). iOS — the tab bar is the system's.

### The page does not scroll sideways  (no screen of its own; web: `html, body` in `app/assets/css/main.css`; `wrap-anywhere` on the free text of `app/pages/book/[key].vue`)
Purpose: on Android (Chrome and the installed app) the page could be moved a little left and right and showed the track of a horizontal scroll bar at the bottom while it scrolled: something was wider than the screen.
Rules: (1) No main screen is wider than the viewport (`scrollWidth <= clientWidth`), at 360 px and wider, in both themes. (2) A word wider than the screen in a title, an author line or a description (book page) breaks (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) instead of running out of its box on both sides. (3) As a net, `html` and `body` clip the x axis (`overflow-x: clip`; `hidden` would make `body` a scroll container and end `position: sticky`) and `overscroll-behavior-x: none`: whatever is wider is cut off at the screen's edge, the page never scrolls or bounces sideways, and the browser has no sideways overscroll at the page's edge (history navigation, glow). Up and down are untouched: the pull to refresh, the sticky headers, the safe areas. (4) Rows that scroll sideways (the year cards, the year pills, Up next, Regal's shelf row, edition lists) stay scrollers of their own.
IDs: none new.
Flow: no Playwright flow; on a real device `web/e2e/android/scroll-x.ts` (docs/TESTING.md).
Native: not applicable (a native screen does not scroll sideways unless it is a pager).

### Import offer on Home  (web: `components/home/ImportOffer.vue` on Home and its empty state, `composables/useImportOffer.ts`, `utils/importHint.ts`, `data/importedBooks.ts`; the mark is written by `stores/import.ts`)
Purpose: a new member should not have to look behind Profile → Import books for the way to bring her Goodreads or Hardcover export along (owner's test with a fresh account, after #158).
Rules: (1) **Empty Library** (no entry on any Status): under the search prompt, a quiet row in the install hint's family (`bg-fill`, hairline edge, no pop-up, no modal) that is one link to `/import` (`home.importOfferAction`, a real link): the import icon in a ring, the heading `home.importOffer.title` ("Coming from Goodreads or Hardcover?", an `h2`), one line `home.importOffer.text` under it, and a chevron, as a Profile row. The search path stays; the lamp is drawn at half its height while the card shows, like under the install hint. No ×. (2) **A few entries** (one to three over all Statuses, nothing imported): the same row, with `home.importOffer.action` ("Import books") as its line, above *Currently reading*, under the install hint, with a × (`home.importOffer.dismiss`, 44 pt target) that hides it for good on this device. (3) **Never** to a member who imported before (this device remembers `imported`, or the one question below found an import key), never to one with a real Library (more than three entries), never again after the × over a few entries (an emptied Library gets the whole card again unless she imported). (4) **What the device remembers** is per member, a map in `libellus-import-hint` (member id → `dismissed` | `imported`), outside the `libellus.` prefix so signing out keeps it. `imported` is written when an import wrote books (`stores/import.ts`) and cannot be taken back by a dismissal. (5) **No layout shift.** The card is decided synchronously from the Library as the device holds it (`useLibraryStore` restores it before the first frame) and the device's memory, so it is in the first frame or not at all. The one thing the device may not know — an import made on another device, with at most three entries here — is asked once per start (`hasImportedBooks`: any entry with an `import_key`; offline or failing, dropped); a yes is remembered and the small card closes like a `Reveal` (`quick` fade without travel under Reduce Motion). Dismissing closes it the same way.
Copy keys: `home.importOffer.title|text|action|dismiss`
IDs: `home.importOffer`, `home.importOfferTitle`, `home.importOfferText`, `home.importOfferAction`, `home.importOfferDismiss`
Flow: no flow; `web/tests/import-hint.test.ts` (the rules and the device memory) and `web/tests/imported-books.test.ts` (an entry with an import key: asked of the database). Every other flow starts with the member marked `imported` (`signedIn` in `e2e/support.ts`), like the install hint.
Native: the same rules; the memory is the app's own preference store, per member.

### Install hint  (web: `components/home/InstallHint.vue` on Home and its empty state, `composables/useInstallHint.ts`, `plugins/install.client.ts`, `utils/installHint.ts`; the Install app row in `components/profile/Account.vue`)
Purpose (#94): iOS has no install prompt, so a member has to know Safari's Share → Add to Home Screen. Home says so once, quietly; on Android the browser's own install is offered from the Profile.
Rules: (1) **Where it shows.** Only in Safari on an iPhone, iPod or iPad — an iPad that asks for the desktop site says it is a Mac and is told apart by `maxTouchPoints > 1` — and only while Libellus is not installed (`navigator.standalone` is not true and `display-mode: standalone` does not match). Other iOS browsers (Chrome, Firefox, Edge, in-app web views) lay their Share menu out differently, so they get nothing; Android and desktop never see it. (2) **What it is.** A calm card above Home's content (under the greeting; above the lamp on the empty Home, where the lamp is drawn at half its height while the hint shows, and not at all on a phone shorter than 760 px, so the search prompt stays above the tab bar; unchanged without the hint): `home.installHint.title`, one line (`home.installHint.text`) and a tiny drawing of what Safari shows — its Share button, then the "Add to Home Screen" row (`home.installHint.addToHome`) — in the text colours, so it follows both themes. (3) **Dismissal.** Its × (`home.installHintDismiss`) hides it and stores the time on this device (`libellus-install-hint`, outside the `libellus.` prefix, so signing out keeps it): it stays away for seven days and then asks once more; never more than once a week. Once installed, the installed app never shows it (the Home Screen app's storage is separate from Safari's, so the Safari tab keeps following the weekly rule). (4) **Android.** Chrome offers its own install; the Profile's account rows add a quiet "Install app" row (`profile.install`) only after Chrome fired `beforeinstallprompt` (kept from boot by `plugins/install.client.ts`); tapping it shows Chrome's dialog, and the row is gone once the event is spent or the app installed. No event (iOS, desktop Safari, the installed app, an already installed Chrome) → no row.
IDs: `home.installHint`, `home.installHintTitle`, `home.installHintText`, `home.installHintArt`, `home.installHintDismiss`, `profile.install`.
Flow: `web/tests/install-hint.test.ts` (the detection and weekly rules; no flow). Real device: the iOS Simulator's Safari (hint, Share menu, Add to Home Screen, and the installed app without the hint).
Native: nothing to port (a native app has nothing to install).

### What's new  (web: `components/shell/WhatsNewSheet.vue` in `app/layouts/tabs.vue`, `composables/useWhatsNew.ts`, `utils/changelog.ts`, `appConfig.release` in `nuxt.config.ts`, the Version line under the rows of `components/profile/Account.vue`; the source: `CHANGELOG.md` and `version.txt` at the repository root, written by release-please, docs/OPERATIONS.md "Releases")
Purpose: after an update, a member learns what changed in words she uses, once; the release and its notes stay one tap away on the Profile.
Rules: (1) **What it holds.** The build embeds its release (`version.txt`) and only that release's part of `CHANGELOG.md`. Kept: *Changed* (breaking changes), *New* (features), *Faster* (performance), *Fixed* (fixes), in that order; never docs, tests, CI, chores, refactors or reverts. Each line is the commit's subject as a sentence: no `type(scope):`, no `**scope:**`, no links, commit or issue numbers, the first letter raised, repeats dropped. (2) **When it comes by itself.** Once per device, in the signed-in shell, when the device last saw an older release (`libellus:whatsNew`, outside the `libellus.` prefix, so signing out keeps it) and this one has something *New* or *Changed*. A release of fixes or speed alone does not interrupt. A device that never saw a release (a new one, or one older than What's new) only starts counting, silently. Not over a shortcut or share the app was opened with (any query on the address). The version is remembered when the shell starts, so a reload never brings the sheet back. (3) **On the Profile.** Under the account rows, a quiet centred line `Version 1.9.0 · What's new` (mono caption, the link part in the lamp's ink, a 44 pt target) opens the same sheet any time. (4) **The sheet.** Title "What's new in 1.9" (major.minor), *Done* at the right closes it (as do Cancel, the scrim, Escape, a swipe, Back); a mono `Version 1.9.0` line, then each section's eyebrow and its sentences behind small lamp-coloured dots; a release with none of the kept sections says `whatsNew.none`. Nothing in it writes; it works offline.
States: content · none (no kept sections) · hidden (a build without `version.txt`: no line, never a sheet).
Copy keys: `whatsNew.title`, `whatsNew.version`, `whatsNew.done`, `whatsNew.none`, `whatsNew.section.{breaking,features,performance,fixes}`, `profile.account.version`, `profile.account.whatsNew`.
IDs: `whatsNew` (+ `.sheetTitle`, `.action`, `.cancel`), `whatsNew.version`, `whatsNew.section.<kind>`, `whatsNew.item`, `whatsNew.none`, `profile.whatsNew`.
Flow: `web/tests/changelog.test.ts` (reading release-please's changelog, the friendly lines, when the sheet comes by itself; no flow).
Native: the same rules over the same embedded notes (the build reads the changelog, the app never fetches it); the last seen version in the platform's key-value store.

### Core loop (smoke)  (every screen above, in one session; `web/e2e/core-loop.spec.ts`)
Purpose: the loop Libellus promises, checked as one flow on every pull request: find a Book, read it, rate it, see it counted.
Flow: the Playwright flow signs a fresh member in through the screens, then:
1. Sign in (`signIn.email` → `signIn.submit` → `verify.code`, the code read from Mailpit) → Home, the empty state (`home.emptyTitle`).
2. Search (`shell.tab.search` → `search.query` "Piranesi" → `search.result`) → the book page (`book.title`).
3. Add (`book.add` → `add.submit`) → `book.status` shows Want to read.
4. Start (`book.start` → `start.submit`) → `book.status` shows Currently reading.
5. Finish (`book.updateProgress` → `progress.finish` → `finish.rating`, four stars → `finish.review` → `finish.submit`) → `book.status` shows Finished with `book.rating`.
6. Home (`shell.tab.home`) → `home.tallyCount` is 1 under `home.tallyLabel`, `home.readingCard` is gone.
7. Library (`shell.tab.library` → `library.segment.finished`) → the year (`library.yearTitle`) with the Book (`library.entryTitle`) and its Rating (`library.entryRating`).
The session stored in the database has the Rating in quarters (16) and the review as typed.
Test IDs everywhere: **every control a member can reach has a `data-testid` of the form `<screen>.<element>`**. The Playwright fixture (`web/e2e/fixtures.ts`) watches every page of every flow and fails the test on a button, link, field or other control that shows without one.
No live APIs: Apple, OpenLibrary and their cover CDNs answer from the recordings (`web/tests/fixtures`, wired in `web/e2e/support.ts`); the same fixture refuses a request to any other host and fails the test, so a flow can never call a live service. Supabase is the local stack, fresh in CI; flows never assume what the shared Catalogue holds (they stub `search_books` where an answer from it would change the list).
In CI (`.github/workflows/e2e.yml`, run by a release, the `full-e2e` label and by hand): the pinned Supabase CLI starts the stack, WebKit is installed with its system libraries (the download is cached per Playwright version), `pnpm e2e --shard=i/3` runs on an iPhone 15 viewport with two workers and one retry, in three shards on three runners (each with its own fresh stack; #105), and when a shard fails its blob report is uploaded and the `e2e-report` job merges them into one HTML report with the traces, the `playwright-report` artifact.
Native: Maestro (or XCUITest) flows follow the same seven steps with the same IDs as `accessibilityIdentifier`s.
- [x] Web  - [ ] iOS  - [ ] Android
